c_{0}({\mathbf{N}},L)\widehat{\otimes}_{L}M_{n}\simeq c_{0}({\mathbf{N}},M_{n})
.
Therefore we may rewrite any

\mathcal{C}^{\rm{la}}(X,M)\simeq\varinjlim_{\{X_{i}\}}\prod_{i}\varinjlim_{n}c_{0}(X,M_{n}).

However, by
[
Eme17
, 2.1.6]
we have a natural continuous bijection
\varinjlim_{n}c_{0}(X,M_{n})\to c_{0}(X,M)
(the
M_{n}
are envelopes for the so-called
BH
-subspaces of
M
). It is not clear if this map is a homeomorphism, but we are only interested in the vector space structure. In particular, since filtered colimits and finite products are exact on vector spaces, it suffices to show the exactness of the sequence

0\to c_{0}({\mathbf{N}},U)\to c_{0}({\mathbf{N}},V)\to c_{0}({\mathbf{N}},W)\to 0.

But such an exactness is clear.
∎

The relevance of this result is to show exactness of the functor
I_{\leq k}
, as follows. First note that the projection map
\widetilde{G}\to\overline{P}\backslash\widetilde{G}
has a locally analytic section
s:\overline{P}\backslash\widetilde{G}\to\widetilde{G}
, and thus as topological vector spaces, we have an isomorphism
\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}}^{\widetilde{G}}\pi\simeq\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\rm{la}}(s(\overline{P}\backslash\widetilde{G}),\pi)
.
For an arbitrary open subset
\Omega\subset\overline{P}\backslash\widetilde{G}
this isomorphism induces isomorphisms
I_{\Omega}\pi\simeq\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\rm{la}}(\Omega,\pi)
. As a result,
I_{k}(\pi)=\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\rm{la}}(\overline{P}\backslash(\overline{P}\cdot\widetilde{G}_{k}),\pi)
.
We can then prove the following:

Lemma 3.6
.

All the functors
I_{\leq k},

I_{w}
send strict exact sequences in
\operatorname{Rep}_{\rm{la}.c}(G)
to strict exact sequences in
\operatorname{Rep}_{\rm{la.c}}(B)
. Moreover for all
k
we have a strict exact sequence

0\to I_{\leq k}(\pi)\to I_{\leq k+1}(\pi)\to I_{w_{k+1}}(\pi)\to 0.

(14)

Proof.

I_{\leq k}
is exact by
Lemma

3.5
combined with the above discussion.
Likewise,
I_{w}
is a filtered colimit of
I_{V}\pi
for
V
compact open, which is exact. All these sequences are strict since the spaces in discussion are vector spaces of compact type, and the first maps will then be closed embeddings.

For showing (
14
), the first map is clearly a closed embedding and the second map is clearly surjective. For exactness in the middle, suppose
f\in I_{\leq k+1}(\pi)
maps to zero in
I_{w_{k+1}}\pi
. By definition, for some compact open neighborhood
U_{w_{k+1},\underline{x}}\subseteq\overline{P}\backslash\widetilde{G}
we have
f|_{U_{w_{k+1},\underline{x}}}=0
. Thus,
\operatorname{supp}f\subset(U_{w_{k+1},\underline{x}})^{c}
. Since
U_{w_{k+1},\underline{x}}\supset\overline{P}w_{k+1}N
, we have
\overline{P}w_{k+1}N\cap\operatorname{supp}f\subseteq\left(\overline{P}w_{k+1}N\right)\cap(U_{w_{k+1},\underline{x}})^{c}=\emptyset
.

This emptyness implies
\operatorname{supp}f\subset\overline{P}\backslash\overline{P}\widetilde{G}_{k}
, so the kernel is contained in the image. Conversely if
f\in I_{\leq k}(\pi)
then
\operatorname{supp}f\subset\widetilde{G}_{k}
is a compact open subset, and thus the complement
(\operatorname{supp}f)^{c}
is also compact open. In particular, there exists an open neighborhood of
\overline{P}w_{k+1}N
on which
f=0
, and thus the image is contained in the kernel, proving exactness.

Clearly these maps are continuous. These maps are all of compact type spaces, so open mapping theorem implies these maps are strict.
∎

We want to make sense of these definitions on the level of complexes. While we defined
I_{\leq k},I_{w}
for representations of
\overline{P}
, for complexes it is advantageous to work with compact groups. Thus, let
\widetilde{G}_{0}:=\widetilde{G}({\mathcal{O}}_{F,p})
as well as
B_{0}=B\cap\widetilde{G}_{0}=T_{0}N_{0}
,
\overline{P}_{0}=\overline{P}\cap\widetilde{G}_{0}=G_{0}\overline{U}_{0}
. Now suppose
\pi
is a locally analytic representation of
\overline{P}_{0}
. Note since
\overline{P}\backslash\widetilde{G}\simeq\overline{P}_{0}\backslash\widetilde{G}_{0}
, for any
U\subset\overline{P}\backslash\widetilde{G}
, we may define
I_{\leq k}\pi:=(\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\widetilde{G}_{0}}\pi)(\widetilde{G}_{\leq k})
, and similarly
I_{w}\pi
. Note if
\pi
is in fact a locally analytic representation of
\overline{P}
, then there is a
\widetilde{G}_{0}
-equivariant isomorphism
\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}}^{\widetilde{G}}\pi\simeq\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\widetilde{G}_{0}}\pi
which induces isomorphisms on
I_{\leq k},I_{w}
, so these definitions are unambiguous. Now suppose
\Pi
is a complex of injective admissible Banach representations of
\overline{P}_{0}
. We can form complexes
I_{\leq k}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}},I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}
. Then we have the following:

Lemma 3.7
.

If
(\widetilde{\Pi},i,p)
is a
\overline{P}
-extension of
\Pi
, then each functor
\mathsf{F}=I_{\leq k},I_{w}
induces
B
-extensions
\hbox to100.68pt{\vbox to20.94pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 50.33875pt\lower-13.03662pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.08894pt}{-9.52277pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -1.51 -13.18)} \pgfsys@hbox{62}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},
i.e. homotopy equivalences to a complex of compact type spaces with a locally analytic action of
B
. Moreover, these homotopy equivalences fit into a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\leq k}\widetilde{\Pi}^{\operatorname{la}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\leq k+1}\widetilde{\Pi}^{\operatorname{la}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{w_{k+1}}\widetilde{\Pi}^{\operatorname{la}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\leq k}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\leq k+1}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{w_{k+1}}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{i}}

(15)

where we have
\widetilde{i}\circ f=\widetilde{f}\circ\widetilde{i}
,
\widetilde{p}\circ\widetilde{f}=f\circ\widetilde{p}
,
\widetilde{i}\circ g=\widetilde{g}\circ\widetilde{i}
,
\widetilde{p}\circ\widetilde{g}=g\circ\widetilde{p}
, respectively.

Proof.

This result more or less follows from
[
Fu22b
, Proposition 3.13]
.

Let
\mathsf{F}
be any of the functors
I_{\leq k},I_{w}
. Each of these functors are built from spaces of the form
I_{K}\pi
for
K\subseteq\overline{P}_{0}\backslash\widetilde{G}_{0}=\overline{P}\backslash\widetilde{G}
open (compact or not). Then from
[
Fu22b
, Proposition 3.13]
we have a
\widetilde{G}
-extension

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\widetilde{G}_{0}}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}}^{\widetilde{G}}\widetilde{\Pi}^{\operatorname{la}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{p}}

where concretely, if
f\in\operatorname{Ind}_{P_{0}}^{\widetilde{G}_{0}}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}
then
\widetilde{i}(f)=:f^{\prime}
is the unique function such that
f^{\prime}(g)=i(f(g))
for
i:\Pi\to\widetilde{\Pi}
and
g\in\widetilde{G}_{0}
, and
\widetilde{p}(f^{\prime})=:f
satisfies
f(g)=p(f^{\prime}(g))
for
p:C^{\prime}\to C
and any
g\in\widetilde{G}_{0}
. We can now contemplate support conditions in this
\widetilde{G}
-extension. If
\operatorname{supp}f\subset K\subset\overline{P}_{0}\backslash\widetilde{G}_{0}
then clearly we have
\operatorname{supp}\widetilde{i}(f)\subseteq\overline{P}\backslash(\overline{P}\cdot K)\subseteq\overline{P}\backslash\widetilde{G}
. But we then simply note that under the identification
\overline{P}_{0}\backslash\widetilde{G}_{0}\simeq\overline{P}\backslash\widetilde{G}
, we have
K\simeq\overline{P}\backslash(\overline{P}\cdot K)
, so
\widetilde{i}(I_{K}\Pi)\subset I_{K}\widetilde{\Pi}
. Likewise, if
f^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}}^{\widetilde{G}}C_{\bullet}^{\prime}
has support
K\subseteq\overline{P}\backslash\widetilde{G}
then
\widetilde{p}(f^{\prime})
has support contained in
\overline{P}\backslash\overline{P}_{0}\cdot K\cap\widetilde{G}_{0}\simeq\overline{P}\backslash\overline{P}\cdot K
when considered as a subset of the flag variety
\overline{P}_{0}\backslash\widetilde{G}_{0}
. Thus if
U\subseteq\overline{P}\backslash\widetilde{G}
is an open invariant under the right action by the Borel
B
, then the
\widetilde{G}
-extension on the parabolic induction induces
B_{0}
-equivariant homotopy equivalences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\widetilde{G}_{0}}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}}^{\widetilde{G}}\widetilde{\Pi}^{\operatorname{la}}(U).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{p}}

This handles the case of
I_{\leq k}
. For the
I_{w}
recalling
I_{w}\pi=\varinjlim_{\underline{x}}I_{U_{w,\underline{x}}}\pi
, the above maps are not necessarily
B
-equivariant for a given
U_{w,\underline{x}}
, but we still have well-defined homotopy equivalences. Then we get an induced homotopy equivalence on the colimits, which a postiori are
B_{0}
-equivariant, and thus produces the relevant “
B
-extension.”

The existence and commutativity of the diagram follows directly from the fact that all of the homotopies are induced from the single choice of homotopy equivalence between
\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\widetilde{G}_{0}}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}
and
\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}}^{\widetilde{G}}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}
.
∎

Crucially,
Lemma

3.7
allows us to define derived Jacquet functors
J_{B}(I_{\leq k}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})
and
J_{B}(I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})
. Moreover, the commutativity of the squares in (
15
)
on the nose
(not just up to homotopy!) constructs for us natural maps
J_{B}(I_{\leq k}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})\to J_{B}(I_{\leq k+1}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})\to J_{B}(I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})
. We would like to say more about these maps (and their effect on cohomology). These features are best understood by first passing to
\mathfrak{n}
-invariants where
\mathfrak{n}:=\operatorname{Lie}N
is the Lie algebra of the unipotent radical
N\subset B.

The key is the following lemma:

Lemma 3.8
.

For any two compact open subsets
U\subset V\subset\overline{P}\backslash\widetilde{G}
and any Lie subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{g}
we have that

\mathrm{H}^{i}(\mathfrak{h},I_{U}(\pi))\hookrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{i}(\mathfrak{h},I_{V}(\pi))

is an injection.

Proof.

Note that if
U\subset\overline{P}\backslash\widetilde{G}
is compact open then the complement
U^{c}
is also compact and open, and thus we can write any function
f=f|_{U}+f|_{U^{c}}
uniquely as a sum of two functions each support on
U
and
U^{c}
, respectively. The Lie algebra action does not affect the support, so we have a
\mathfrak{g}
-equivariant decomposition
I_{V}\pi\simeq I_{U}\pi\oplus I_{V\setminus U}\pi
. Passing to
\mathfrak{h}
-cohomology gives the desired result.
∎

Corollary 3.9
.

Let
\pi
be an injective admissible Banach
\overline{P}_{0}
-representation. Then for all
k\geq 0
and all
w\in W_{\overline{P}}^{r}
we have
I_{\leq k}\pi^{\operatorname{la}}
and
I_{w}\pi^{\operatorname{la}}
are
R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{h}}
-acyclic for any Lie subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{g}
.

Proof.

Since
\pi
is injective,
\pi
is a direct summand of
\mathcal{C}(\overline{P}_{0},L)^{\oplus d}
for some
d\geq 0
. Thus
\operatorname{ct}-\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\widetilde{G}_{0}}\pi
is a direct summand of
\mathcal{C}(\widetilde{G}_{0},L)^{\oplus d}
, and so is also injective admissible. Then recall by
[
Fu22b
, Lemma 3.8]
functor
(-)^{\rm{la}}
sends injectives to
R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{h}}
-acyclic objects. In particular,
\mathrm{H}^{i}(\mathfrak{n},\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\widetilde{G}_{0}}\pi^{\operatorname{la}})=0
for all
i\geq 1
. But since taking Lie algebra cohomology commutes with colimits in the coefficients, for both
I_{\leq k}
and
I_{w}
it suffices to show
I_{U}(\pi)
is
R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{h}}
-acyclic for any
U\subset\overline{P}_{0}\backslash\widetilde{G}_{0}
compact open. But this case then follows from
Lemma

3.8
.
∎

The following obvious lemma connects
\mathfrak{n}
-cohomology to an object closer to the Jacquet functor.

Lemma 3.10
.

For
V
a complex of locally analytic representations of
B_{0}
with a
B
-extension, we have a
T^{+}
-equivariant topological isomorphism of topological vector spaces

\mathrm{H}^{i}(V^{N_{0}})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{i}(V^{\mathfrak{n}})^{N_{0}}

Proof.

Since
N_{0}
-invariants is abstractly exact on smooth
N_{0}
-representations over characteristic
0
fields, the natural map

\mathrm{H}^{i}(V^{N_{0}})\to\mathrm{H}^{i}(V^{\mathfrak{n}})^{N_{0}}
is a
T^{+}
-equivariant continuous bijection. Moreover, since the maps
V^{i,N_{0}}\hookrightarrow V^{i,\mathfrak{n}}
are in fact closed embeddings, by the definition of the quotient topology, we have that the image of a closed set is automatically closed, and thus the inverse is also continuous.
∎

Lemma 3.11
.

Let
\Pi
be a bounded complex of injective admissible
\overline{P}_{0}
-representations, equipped with a
\overline{P}
-extension
(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\bullet},i,p)
. Then
J_{B}(I_{\leq k}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}),J_{B}(I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})
are complexes of essentially admissible representations of
Y=\langle z^{\pm 1},T_{0}\rangle
, and the cohomology groups
\mathrm{H}^{\ast}(J_{B}(I_{\leq k}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})),\mathrm{H}^{\ast}(J_{B}(I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}))
are essentially admissible representations of
T(F_{p}^{+})
which are independent of the choice of
\overline{P}
-extension. Moreover, for all
m
we have a short exact sequence of essentially admissible
T(F_{p}^{+})
-representations

0\to\mathrm{H}^{m}(J_{B}(I_{\leq k}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}))\to\mathrm{H}^{m}(J_{B}(I_{\leq k+1}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}))\to\mathrm{H}^{m}(J_{B}(I_{w_{k+1}}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}))\to 0.

Proof.

From (
14
) we have a short exact sequence of complexes,

0\to I_{\leq k}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}\to I_{\leq k+1}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}\to I_{w_{k+1}}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}\to 0

(16)

which by (
15
) respects the
B
-extensions on each term, and thus is equivariant for the action of
\widetilde{z}:=\widetilde{p}\circ z\circ\widetilde{i}
.

Since each of these functors send injectives to
R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{n}}
-acyclic objects, exactness of
N_{0}
-invariants on smooth representations gives in each degree short exact sequences

0\to(I_{\leq k}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{N_{0}}\to(I_{\leq k+1}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{N_{0}}\to(I_{w_{k+1}}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{N_{0}}\to 0

(17)

We can then take the finite slope part of each term with respect to
\widetilde{z}
, which is an exact operation by
[
Fu22b
, Theorem 1.1]
, so we get a short exact sequence of complexes of
Y
-representations

0\to J_{B}(I_{\leq k}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})\to J_{B}(I_{\leq k+1}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})\to J_{B}(I_{w_{k+1}}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})\to 0.

Since we already know that
J_{B}(\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\widetilde{G}_{0}}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})
is essentially admissible over
Y
, all the other
J_{B}(I_{\leq k}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})
and
J_{B}(I_{w_{k+1}}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})
are as well. The statement about cohomology and independence of choice follows from same proof of
[
Fu22b
, Theorem 4.10]
. For the last statement, by passing to the long exact sequence on cohomology it suffices to show
\mathrm{H}^{m}(J_{B}(I_{\leq k}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}))\to\mathrm{H}^{m}(J_{B}(I_{\leq k+1}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}))
is injective for all
m\geq 0
.

Choosing a representative
f\in I_{\leq k}\Pi^{\operatorname{la},m}
, we contemplate its support
\operatorname{supp}f\subseteq\widetilde{G}_{k}\subset\overline{P}_{0}\backslash\widetilde{G}_{0}
, which is by definition a compact open subset. Now consider the map
\overline{P}_{0}\backslash\widetilde{G}_{0}\times N_{0}\to\overline{P}_{0}\backslash\widetilde{G}_{0}
sending
(\overline{P}_{0}r,n)\mapsto\overline{P}_{0}rn
. This map is visibly continuous and thus sends compact sets to compact sets.

In particular,
(\operatorname{supp}f)N_{0}
is a compact open subset of
\overline{P}_{0}\backslash\widetilde{G}_{0}
, and moreover since
\widetilde{G}_{k}
is left
P
-invariant and right
B
-invariant, we have
U_{f}:=(\operatorname{supp}f)N_{0}\subset\widetilde{G}_{k}
. In particular, this implies

I_{\leq k}\Pi^{\operatorname{la},m}=\varinjlim_{\begin{subarray}{c}U\subset\widetilde{G}_{k}\\
\text{ compact open}\end{subarray}}I_{U}(\Pi^{\operatorname{la},m})=\varinjlim_{\begin{subarray}{c}UN_{0}\subset\widetilde{G}_{k}\\
\text{compact open}\end{subarray}}I_{UN_{0}}(\Pi^{\operatorname{la},m}).

Moreover for any such
UN_{0}
an open compact which is left
\overline{P}
-invariant and right
N_{0}
-invariant, we have the indicator function
\mathbf{1}_{U}:G\to L
is locally constant (and thus locally analytic). Thus we have a decomposition as
N_{0}
-representations
I_{\leq k+1}\Pi^{\operatorname{la},m}\simeq I_{U}\Pi^{\operatorname{la},m}\oplus I_{\widetilde{G}_{k}-U}\Pi^{\operatorname{la},m}
. Then passing to the colimit above yields a continuous injection
\mathrm{H}^{i}((I_{\leq k}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{N_{0}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{i}((I_{\leq k+1}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{N_{0}})
. Moreover, the action of the operator
U_{z}
for
z\in T^{++}

shrinks
the support, and thus
(I_{U}\Pi^{\operatorname{la},m})^{N_{0}}
is stable under the
U_{z}
-operator. Since
(I_{\leq k}\Pi^{\operatorname{la},m})^{N_{0}}\hookrightarrow(I_{\leq k+1}\Pi^{\operatorname{la},m})^{N_{0}}
is a closed embedding of spaces of compact type, the induced injection on cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{i}((I_{\leq k}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{N_{0}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{i}((I_{\leq k+1}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{N_{0}})
is continuous, closed, and equivariant for the action of the operator
U_{z}
! Thus, we can pass to a Hausdorff quotients and take the finite slope parts (both which will preserve this continuous closed injection) to get

\mathrm{H}^{m}(J_{B}(I_{\leq k}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}))\hookrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{m}(J_{B}(I_{\leq k+1}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})),

yielding the desired result.
∎

4
Degree shifting

For this section, we specialize to the setup
\widetilde{G}=\widetilde{G}(F_{p}^{+})\simeq\operatorname{GL}_{2n}(F_{p})
,
G=G(F_{p}^{+})\simeq\operatorname{GL}_{n}(F_{p})\times\operatorname{GL}_{n}(F_{p})
and
G\subset\overline{P}
the (opposite) Siegel parabolic.
Then we let
\Pi=\Pi_{\overline{P}}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
be a bounded complex of injective admissible
\overline{P}_{0}
-representations equipped with
\overline{P}
-extension, computing the completed cohomology of
\overline{P}
localized at
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}=S^{\ast}(\mathfrak{m})
. Moreover,
\Pi
has a
\overline{P}
-equivariant quasi-isomorphism from
\operatorname{Inf}_{G_{0}}^{\overline{P}_{0}}\Pi_{G}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}\to\Pi
, where
\Pi_{G}(K^{p})
is a bounded complex of injective admissible
G_{0}
-representations, and this quasi-isomorphism is compatible with homotopies from complexes with actions of
\overline{P}
.

In light of
Lemma

3.11
, the goal now is to isolate out a subquotient of
\mathrm{H}^{i}(J_{B}(I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}))
, which resembles a (
w
-twisted) derived Jacquet module of
\Pi_{G}(K^{p})^{\operatorname{la}}
in a different degree. The main result of this section is
Theorem

4.10
, from which we get consequences about Hecke algebras, and thus eigenvarieties.

4.1
Lie algebra cohomology

We first perform many arguments on the level of Lie algebra cohomology. Recall by
Corollary

3.9
we have for any
\widetilde{G}_{0}
-representation
V
such that
V|_{H}\simeq\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{la}}(H,L)^{\oplus m_{i}}
for
H\subset\widetilde{G}_{0}
a compact open subgroup that
I_{w}V
is
R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{n}}
-acyclic, so the complex

(I_{w}\Pi^{\rm{la}})^{\mathfrak{n}}=R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{n}}(I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}),

equals the derived
\mathfrak{n}
-invariants,
at least in the derived category of abstract
L[T_{0}]
-modules. Moreover, the auxiliary choice of
\overline{P}
-extension on
\Pi
equips these cohomology groups with an action of the entire torus
T
. We equip the cohomology groups
\mathrm{H}^{\ast}((I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{\mathfrak{n}})
with the (sub)quotient topologies induced from the modules
(I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{\mathfrak{n}}
.

In particular, the cohomology groups
\mathrm{H}^{\ast}((I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{\mathfrak{n}})
are independent (even topologically) of the choice of representing complex
\Pi
of injective admissible Banach
\overline{P}_{0}
-representations by
Corollary

3.9
. Although we will manage to avoid working systematically with derived categories of topological modules, it will be crucial for us to compute this Lie algebra cohomology in another way, using Chevalley–Eilenberg complexes.

Recall that given a Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
over
L
and an
L[\mathfrak{g}]
-module
M
, that the associated
Chevalley–Eilenberg complex

\operatorname{CE}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M):=\{M\to\bigwedge^{1}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}\otimes_{L}M\to\bigwedge^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}\otimes_{L}M\to\dots\}

is a finite complex computing
\mathrm{H}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{g},M)
. Recalling
\bigwedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}\otimes_{L}M\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{L}(\bigwedge^{k}\mathfrak{g},M)
can be thought of as alternating multilinear functions on
\mathfrak{g}
, the differentials
d_{\operatorname{CE}}^{k}:\bigwedge^{k}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}\otimes_{L}M\to\bigwedge^{k+1}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}\otimes_{L}M
are given by the formula

(d_{\operatorname{CE}}^{k}f)(X_{1},\dots,X_{k+1}):=\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}X_{i}\cdot f(\dots,\widehat{X_{i}},\dots)+\sum_{i,j}(-1)^{i+j}[X_{i},X_{j}]\cdot f([X_{i},X_{j}],X_{1},\dots,\widehat{X_{i}},\dots\widehat{X_{j}},\dots).

Note that this complex essentially arises from a version of the Koszul resolution of the trivial
\mathfrak{g}
-module
\mathbf{1}
, and applying
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathfrak{g}}(-,M)
to this resolution.

In particular for
any
complex of
\mathfrak{g}
-modules
C^{\bullet}
, the total complex of the double complex
\operatorname{CE}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\bullet_{1}}(C^{\bullet_{2}}):=\bigwedge^{\bullet_{1}}\mathfrak{g}^{\ast}\otimes_{L}C^{\bullet_{2}}
computes the derived functors
R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{g}}(C^{\bullet})
. We may also equip such cohomology groups with the subquotient topologies arising from this total complex. In the case of a complex of
R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{g}}
-acyclic objects, we note the following lemma about topological cohomology:

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
V^{\bullet}
be either: a) a complex of admissible locally analytic
\widetilde{G}_{0}
-representations of the form
\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{la}}(H,L)^{\oplus m_{\bullet}}
for some compact open subgroup
H\subset\widetilde{G}_{0}
, OR b) a complex of the form
V^{\bullet}\simeq I_{\leq k}C^{\bullet,\operatorname{la}},I_{w}C^{\bullet,\operatorname{la}}
for
C^{\bullet}
a complex of admissible
\overline{P}_{0}
-representations of the form
\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{la}}(\overline{P}_{H},L)^{\oplus m_{\bullet}}
for
\overline{P}_{H}\subset\overline{P}_{0}
a compact open subgroup. Then the natural map of complexes

V^{\mathfrak{n}}\to\operatorname{Tot}(\bigwedge^{\,\,\,\bullet_{1}}\mathfrak{n}^{\ast}\otimes_{L}V^{\bullet_{2}})

coming from the inclusion(s)
V^{\mathfrak{n}}\hookrightarrow V
induces an isomorphism

\mathrm{H}^{r}(V^{\mathfrak{n}})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{r}(\operatorname{Tot}(\bigwedge^{\,\,\,\bullet_{1}}\mathfrak{n}^{\ast}\otimes_{L}V^{\bullet_{2}})),

of topological vector spaces with continuous
T_{0}
-action,
where we endow both sides with the induced subquotient topologies.

Moreover, if
V^{\bullet}
is equipped with a
\widetilde{G}
-extension (or
C^{\bullet}
-equipped with a
\overline{P}
-extension), then these isomorphisms are in fact
T
-equivariant.

Proof.

In either case
V
is a complex of
R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{n}}
-acyclic modules by
Corollary

3.9
, so we have an isomorphism of abstract vector spaces as in the lemma statement induced by the described map. It remains to verify topological properties. Now let
d_{V^{\mathfrak{n}}}^{r},d_{\operatorname{CE}}^{r}
denote the differentials in the
r
th entry of
V^{\mathfrak{n}}
and
\operatorname{Tot}(\bigwedge^{\bullet_{1}}\mathfrak{n}^{\ast}\otimes V^{\bullet_{2},\mathfrak{n}})
, respectively. These differentials are both naturally continuous and
T_{0}
-linear. Since the topologies on both cohomology groups are induced subquotient topologies, the bijection
\mathrm{H}^{r}(V^{\mathfrak{n}})\to\mathrm{H}^{r}(\operatorname{Tot}(\bigwedge^{\bullet_{1}}\mathfrak{n}^{\ast}\otimes V^{\bullet_{2}}))
is continuous and closed if and only if the maps
\ker(d_{\Pi^{\mathfrak{n}}}^{r})\to\ker(d_{\operatorname{CE}}^{r})
are continuous and closed, which is in turn true if and only if the composite map
\ker(d_{V^{\mathfrak{n}}}^{r})\to V^{r,\mathfrak{n}}\to\bigoplus_{i+j=r}\bigwedge^{i}\mathfrak{n}^{\ast}\otimes_{L}V^{j,\mathfrak{n}}
is continuous and closed. But since invariance is a closed condition and the map
d_{V^{\mathfrak{n}}}^{r}
is a continuous map of spaces of compact type, this composite map remains a closed embedding. In particular, it is continuous and also maps closed subsets to closed subsets. Thus the induced bijective map on cohomology is a continuous bijection mapping closed sets to closed sets, and thus is also open.

The final statement regarding
\widetilde{G}
-extensions or
\overline{P}
-extensions is clear upon noting that the map
V^{n}\to V
preserves the action of
T
(up to homotopy).

∎

4.3
Constant term maps

Now for
w\in W^{\overline{P}}
, let
N_{w}:=wN(\mathcal{O}_{F^{+},p})w^{-1}\cap\overline{P}(F_{p}^{+})
and
\mathfrak{n}_{w}:=\operatorname{Lie}N_{w}
.
Let
(A^{\bullet},i,p)
be a choice of
\overline{P}
-extension for
\Pi
. Then we may consider the complex
\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{w}}
, which using the
\overline{P}
-extension structure and some
z\in T^{++}
carries an action of the monoid
Y^{+}=\langle T_{0},z\rangle
given by the formula

\displaystyle t\cdot v:=\sum_{n\in N_{w}/t^{w}N_{w}(t^{w})^{-1}}nt^{w}v,

(18)

where we notate
t^{w}:=wtw^{-1}
. As per usual, this action depends on the choice of
\overline{P}
-extension, but action on the cohomology groups does not. Note also locally analytic vectors of an injective Banach object is
\mathfrak{n}_{w}
-acyclic by
[
Fu22b
, Lemma 3.8]
, so this definition is a reasonable analogue of a topological version of
R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{n}_{w}}(\Pi^{\bullet,\operatorname{la}})
.

For our purposes we use these complexes to define a natural
Y^{+}
-equivariant maps of complexes

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

induced by
f\mapsto f(w)
. These maps are compatible with the homotopy equivalences
(i,p)

on the nose
, since we are simply fixing one choice. The fact that the top horizontal maps intertwine the Atkin–Lehner-like trace operators is proven in
[
ACC+23
, Lemma 5.3.5]
.

Remark 4.4
.

Here lies an important distinction between smooth (
p
-power torsion) and the locally analytic cases: there is no a priori reason that the maps
\operatorname{CT}_{w}
should be surjective (unlike
[
ACC+23
, Lemma 5.3.5]
). The simple issue is that invariant germs of locally analytic inductions may acquire additional relations in their constant terms. Nevertheless, we will salvage the situation by sectioning off a certain “highest weight vector,” and showing surjectivity to this highest weight.

We now want to use
\operatorname{Inf}_{G_{0}}^{\overline{P}_{0}}\Pi_{G}
to compute the
Y^{+}
-representations
\mathrm{H}^{\ast}((\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{\mathfrak{n}_{w}})
. The Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence and a central action gives us a good understanding of these groups. For any
T_{n}(F_{p})
-module
V
let
\tau_{w}V
denote the same underlying space
V
but with twisted action
\tau_{w}(t)(v):=t^{w^{-1}}\cdot v:=(w^{-1}tw)\cdot v
.

Lemma 4.5
.

We have an abstract isomorphism of
T
-representations

\overline{E}:\mathrm{H}^{m}(\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{w}})\simeq\bigoplus_{j=0}^{\ell(w)}\mathrm{H}^{m-j}(R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{n_{n}}}(\tau_{w}^{-1}\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la}}\otimes\tau_{w}^{-1}\mathrm{H}^{j}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1}))).

Moreover, the projection on the final factor induces a continuous
(T^{+},N_{w})
-equivariant surjection

\overline{E}_{\ell(w)}:\mathrm{H}^{m}(\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{w}})\twoheadrightarrow\tau_{w}^{-1}\mathrm{H}^{m-\ell(w)}(\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{n}}\otimes_{L}\mathrm{H}^{\ell(w)}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1})).

Proof.

Let
N_{w}\rtimes_{w}T^{+}
be the the monoid
N_{w}\times T^{+}
equipped with multiplication
(t^{w}n(t^{w})^{-1},1)(1,t)=(1,t)(n,1)
.
To show the decomposition into abstract
L[N_{w}\rtimes_{w}T^{+}]
-modules we use the sequence

0\to\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}}\to\mathfrak{n}_{w}\to\mathfrak{n}_{n}\to 0.

Note that
\mathfrak{n}_{n}
is a quotient of
\mathfrak{n}_{w}
precisely because
w\in W^{\overline{P}}
was chosen to be a minimal length representative.
The Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence for Lie algebra cohomology (using that the modules
\Pi^{\rm{la}}
are acyclic for all the functors involved),
we have as abstract
L[N_{w}\rtimes_{w}Y^{+}]
-modules that

R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{n}_{w}}\Pi^{\rm{la}}\simeq\tau_{w}^{-1}R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{n}_{n}}(R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}}}\Pi^{\rm{la}})\simeq\tau_{w}^{-1}R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{n}_{n}}(\Pi^{\rm{la}}\otimes R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}}}\mathbf{1}),

where the last line follows because the complex
\Pi
is quasi-isomorphic to a complex
\Pi_{G}
inflated from an action of the Levi
G_{0}
. Recall that
G_{0}=G(\mathcal{O}_{F^{+},p})\simeq\operatorname{GL}_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{p}})\times\operatorname{GL}_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{p}})
has centre
\mathcal{O}_{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{F^{+}}}\mathcal{O}_{F^{+},p}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{F_{p}}^{\times}\times\mathcal{O}_{F_{p}}^{\times}
, so we may choose the scalars on each diagonal
n\times n
component separately. Consider the non-torsion element
1+p^{2}\in\mathcal{O}_{F_{\nu}}^{\times}
for all
\nu\mid p
and the corresponding central element
z_{p}=\begin{pmatrix}((1+p^{2}))_{\nu}\cdot I_{n}&0\\
0&I_{n}\end{pmatrix}\in G_{0}
.
This element acts on the cohomology groups
\mathrm{H}^{i}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1})
via conjugation by
z_{p}
on
N_{w,\overline{U}}
. Viewing
N_{w,\overline{U}}\simeq{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}^{\ell(w)}
as a
{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}
-module and considering the Chevalley–Eilenberg complex
\bigwedge^{i}\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}}^{\ast}
,
z_{p}
acts on
\mathrm{H}^{i}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1})
via multiplication by
(1+p^{2})^{i}
. Since these groups are in characteristic zero, all these scalars are distinct, and there is an idempotent
E_{j}:=\prod_{j}\frac{z_{p}-(1+p^{2})^{j}}{(1+p^{2})^{i}-(1+p^{2})^{j}}
on
R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}}}(\mathbf{1})
which induces the identity map on
R^{i}\Gamma_{\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}}}(\mathbf{1})
and zero on all other degrees.
In particular, these maps yield an explicit quasi-isomorphism

\operatorname{CE}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1})\xrightarrow{E}\bigoplus\mathrm{H}^{i}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1})[-i],

(19)

where
E:=\sum_{j=0}^{\ell(w)}E_{j}
. This quasi-isomorphism is naturally equivariant for the action of
T
and
N_{w}
.
Now recall that the
\overline{P}_{0}
-equivariant quasi-isomorphism
\operatorname{Inf}_{G_{0}}^{\overline{P}_{0}}\Pi_{G}\to\Pi
has a continuous
G_{0}
-equivariant homotopy inverse
\Pi\to\operatorname{Inf}_{G_{0}}^{\overline{P}_{0}}\Pi_{G}
, which is also equivariant for the
T
-action up to homotopy.
We can pass to Lie algebra cohomology by considering term-by-term Chevalley–Eilenberg resolutions. In particular, this homotopy equivalence induces another homotopy equivalence

\operatorname{Tot}^{m}(\bigwedge^{\bullet_{1}}\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}}^{\ast}\otimes(\operatorname{Inf}_{G_{0}}^{\overline{P}_{0}}\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la}})^{\bullet_{2}})\to\operatorname{Tot}^{m}(\bigwedge^{\bullet_{1}}\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}}^{\ast}\otimes\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\bullet_{2}}).

Note however that the action of
\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}}
on
\Pi_{G}
is trivial, so the first complex can be rewritten as
\operatorname{CE}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1})\otimes\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la}}
, with
T_{0}
-action given diagonally. Then using (
19
), the sum of idempotents
E
induces an explicit quasi-isomorphism

\operatorname{Tot}^{m}(\operatorname{CE}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1})\otimes\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la}})\simeq\bigoplus_{q=0}^{m}\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la},q}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{m-q}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1}).

In particular, the complex
\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la},\bullet}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{i}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1})[i]
is a direct summand of
(\operatorname{CE}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1})\otimes\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la}})^{n}
.
Then as abstract modules we have
N_{w}\rtimes_{w}T
-equivariant isomorphisms

\overline{E}:R^{m}\Gamma_{\mathfrak{n}_{w}}\operatorname{Inf}_{G_{0}}^{\overline{P}_{0}}\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la}}\simeq\bigoplus_{i=0}^{\ell(w)}\tau_{w}^{-1}R^{m-i}\Gamma_{\mathfrak{n}_{n}}(\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la}}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{i}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1})),

where the twist
\tau_{w}^{-1}
arises from the definition of the action in (
18
).

Now we prove the map
\overline{E}_{\ell(w)}
is continuous. First we claim that the map
\tau_{w}^{-1}\mathrm{H}^{i}(\operatorname{CE}(\mathfrak{n}_{w},\operatorname{Inf}_{G_{0}}^{\overline{P}_{0}}\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la}}))\to\mathrm{H}^{i}(\operatorname{CE}(\mathfrak{n}_{w},\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}))
is a topological isomorphism. A priori, the issue is that
\operatorname{Inf}_{G_{0}}^{\overline{P}_{0}}\Pi_{G}\to\Pi
, only admits a
G_{0}
-equivariant (NOT
\overline{P}_{0}
-equivariant) homotopy inverse, and so a continuous inverse does not immediately exist. However, we do have a topological isomorphism
\mathrm{H}^{i}(\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{w}})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{i}(\operatorname{CE}(\mathfrak{n}_{w},\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}))
induced by the inclusion
\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{w}}\hookrightarrow\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}
, and using the
continuous

G_{0}
-equivariant homotopy inverse, we in fact get a well-defined map of complexes
\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{w}}\to\operatorname{Tot}^{\bullet}(\operatorname{CE}(\mathfrak{n}_{w},\operatorname{Inf}_{G_{0}}^{\overline{P}_{0}}\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la}}))
, which provides a quasi-inverse to the natural map in the other direction. The induced map on cohomology provides a
continuous
inverse.

Note since the
\Pi_{G}^{\bullet}
is a complex of injective admissible representations of
G_{0}
, all the
\Pi_{G}^{\bullet,\operatorname{la}}
are acyclic for
\mathfrak{n}_{n}
-cohomology. However, it is not clear whether the
\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la},\bullet}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{j}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1})
are also since the conjugation action of
\mathfrak{n}_{n}
on
\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}}
is nontrivial. However, by considering top degree forms in
\bigwedge^{\ell(w)}\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}}^{\ast}
, a straightforward calculation shows that the
N_{n}
-action on the
1
-dimensional space
\mathrm{H}^{\ell(w)}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1})
is in fact trivial, so
\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la},\bullet}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{\ell(w)}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1})
is a complex of
R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{n}_{n}}
-acyclic objects, and thus for all
j
we have
\mathrm{H}^{j}((\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la}}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{\ell(w)}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1}))^{\mathfrak{n}_{n}})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{j}(\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{n}}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{\ell(w)}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1}))
.

In particular, by taking
\mathfrak{n}_{n}
-invariants we get a continuous map of complexes
\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{w}}\to(\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{\ell(w)}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},1))^{\mathfrak{n}_{n}}[-\ell(w)]
which on the level of cohomology induces a continuous
N_{w}\rtimes_{w}T
-equivariant surjection

\overline{E}_{\ell(w)}:\mathrm{H}^{m}((\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{\mathfrak{n}_{w}})\to\tau_{w}^{-1}\mathrm{H}^{m-\ell(w)}(\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{n}})\otimes\tau_{w}^{-1}\mathrm{H}^{\ell(w)}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1}),

as required.
∎

To clean up the target of this surjection
\overline{E}_{\ell(w)}
, we first define a character
\chi_{w}:T(F_{p}^{+})\to L^{\times}
via the formula

\chi_{w}(t)=\varepsilon_{\operatorname{cyc}}^{-1}\circ\operatorname{Art}_{F_{p}}\circ\det{}_{F_{p}^{+}}(\operatorname{Ad}(t^{w})|_{\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}}})=\varepsilon_{\operatorname{cyc}}^{-1}\circ\operatorname{Art}_{F_{p}}\circ(w^{-1}w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}\rho^{\widetilde{G}}-w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}\rho^{\widetilde{G}}),

(20)

where
\varepsilon_{\operatorname{cyc}}^{-1}\circ\operatorname{Art}_{F_{p}}:F_{p}^{\times}\to{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
is the map
x\mapsto\prod_{\nu}\varepsilon_{\operatorname{cyc}}^{-1}\circ\operatorname{Art}_{F_{\nu}}(x_{\nu})
.

The reason for introducing this character is that that we have a
T(F_{p}^{+})
-equivariant (respectively
T(F_{p}^{+})^{+}
-equivariant) isomorphism (see
[
Hau16
, Proposition 3.1.8]
)

\tau_{w}^{-1}\mathrm{H}^{\ell(w)}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1})\simeq L(\chi_{w}),\quad\tau_{w}^{-1}\mathrm{H}^{\ell(w)}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1})^{N_{w,\overline{U}}}\simeq L(\chi_{w})

This isomorphism, combined with
Lemma

4.5
and
Section

4.3
gives us a composite continuous map

\mathrm{H}^{i}((I_{w}\Pi^{\rm{la}})^{\mathfrak{n}})\to\mathrm{H}^{i}(\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{w}})\twoheadrightarrow\tau_{w}^{-1}\mathrm{H}^{i-\ell(w)}(\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{n}})\otimes\chi_{w}

(21)

We can use the Chevalley–Eilenberg complexes to give a concrete description of this map as follows: let
X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}}^{\vee},\dots X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{\ell}}^{\vee}
be a choice of basis for the dual Lie algebra
(w\mathfrak{n}w^{-1}\cap\overline{\mathfrak{u}})^{\ast}
, dual to elements
X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}},\dots X_{-r_{\ell}}
generating the Lie algebra, indexed by the (negative) nilpotent root subspaces they generate. Likewise, let
\{Y_{j}^{\vee}\}_{j=1}^{n(n-1)[F_{p}^{+}:{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}]}
be a chosen basis for
\mathfrak{n}_{n}^{\vee}
. Lastly, let
\{Z_{l}\}_{l=1}^{n^{2}[F_{p}^{+}:{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}]-\ell(w)}
be a choice of basis for
(w\mathfrak{n}w^{-1}\cap\mathfrak{u})^{\ast}
. Note that all together, the
X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{i}}^{\vee},Y_{j}^{\vee},Z_{l}^{\vee}
form a basis for the dual Lie algebra
(w\mathfrak{n}w^{-1})^{\ast}
. For convenience, for any subsets of indices
A=\{i_{1},\dots,i_{a}\}\subset\{1,\dots,\dim w\mathfrak{n}w^{-1}\cap\overline{\mathfrak{u}}=\ell(w)\},B=\{j_{1},\dots,j_{b}\}\subset\{1,\dots,\dim\mathfrak{n}_{n}\}
and
C=\{k_{1},\dots,k_{c}\}\subset\{1,\dots,\dim w\mathfrak{n}w^{-1}\cap\mathfrak{u}\}
, we define

L_{A,B,C}:=Z_{C}^{\vee}\wedge Y_{B}^{\vee}\wedge X_{A}^{\vee}:=Z_{k_{1}}^{\vee}\wedge\dots\wedge Z_{k_{c}}^{\vee}\wedge Y_{j_{1}}^{\vee}\wedge\dots\wedge Y_{j_{b}}^{\vee}\wedge X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{i_{1}}}^{\vee}\wedge\dots\wedge X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{i_{a}}}^{\vee}\in\bigwedge^{\,\,\,a+b+c}(w\mathfrak{n}w^{-1})^{\ast}.

Using the total complexes from Chevalley–Eilenberg resolutions of each component, the map

\mathrm{H}^{d}\left(\bigoplus_{i+j=\ast}\bigwedge\mathfrak{n}^{\ast}\otimes I_{w}\Pi^{\rm{la}}\right)\to\mathrm{H}^{d}\left(\bigoplus_{i+j=\ast}\bigwedge\mathfrak{n}_{w}^{\ast}\otimes\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}\right)\xrightarrow{\overline{E}_{\ell(w)}}\tau_{w}^{-1}\mathrm{H}^{d-\ell(w)}\left(\bigoplus_{i+j=\ast}\bigwedge\mathfrak{n}_{n}^{\ast}\otimes\Pi_{G}^{\rm{la}}\right)\otimes\chi_{w}.

sends a cocycle

[\sum_{A,B,C}w^{-1}(L_{A,B,C})\otimes f_{A,B,C}]\mapsto[\sum_{A,B}X_{A}^{\vee}\wedge Y_{B}^{\vee}\otimes f_{A,B,\emptyset}(w)]\mapsto[\sum_{B}Y_{B}^{\vee}\otimes(f_{[\ell(w)],B,\emptyset}(w)\otimes X_{[\ell(w)]}^{\vee}))],

(22)

where
[\ell(w)]:=\{1,\dots,\ell(w)\}
.
This formula for
\overline{E}_{\ell(w)}
follows directly from its construction in the proof of
Lemma

4.5
. In particular, the projection onto this eigenspace kills all terms except those involving the entire basis of
\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}}
. The fact that such an expression remains a cocycle with respect to this quotient Lie algebra is one consequence of the spectral sequence from the proof of
Lemma

4.5
degenerating. The main claim then is as follows:

Proposition 4.6
.

The map

\mathrm{H}^{i}((I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{\mathfrak{n}})\to\tau_{w}^{-1}\mathrm{H}^{i-\ell(w)}(\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{n}})\otimes L(\chi_{w})

is a continuous
T
-equivariant surjection. Moreover, the induced map

\mathrm{H}^{i}((I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{N_{0}})\to\tau_{w}^{-1}\mathrm{H}^{i-\ell(w)}(\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la},N_{n,0}})\otimes L(\chi_{w})

is also continuous, surjective, and equivariant for the action of
T^{+}
.

Proof.

Since the map is by construction continuous it suffices to show the map is surjective as a map of abstract vector spaces.
We proceed by constructing a section
\tau_{w}^{-1}\mathrm{H}^{i-\ell(w)}(\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{n}})\otimes\chi_{w}\to\mathrm{H}^{i}((I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{\mathfrak{n}_{w}})
.

For clarity’s sake, we start with the easiest case of
w=1
. Then the statement to prove is that the constant term map
\mathrm{H}^{d}((\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\widetilde{G}_{0}}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}(\overline{P}_{0}U))^{\mathfrak{n}})\to\mathrm{H}^{d}(\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n_{n}}})
induced by
f\mapsto f(e)
and a homotopy inverse
\Pi\to\Pi_{G}
is surjective. Noting
\mathrm{H}^{d}(\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n_{n}}})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{d}(\Pi_{G}^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{n}})
, it
suffices to show
\mathrm{H}^{d}((\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\widetilde{G}_{0}}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}(\overline{P}_{0}U))^{\mathfrak{n}})\to\mathrm{H}^{d}(\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{n}})
is surjective. But the map
(\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\widetilde{G}_{0}}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}(\overline{P}_{0}U))^{\mathfrak{n}}\to\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{n}}
already has a natural section given by identifying
(\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\widetilde{G}_{0}}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}(\overline{P}_{0}U))^{\mathfrak{n}}\simeq\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{la}}(U,\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{\mathfrak{n}}\simeq\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{sm}}(U,\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{\mathfrak{n}_{n}}
and
\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{n}}\simeq\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{la}}(U,\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{U,\mathfrak{n}_{n}}
, and using the injection of constant functions
\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{la}}(U,\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{U,\mathfrak{n}_{n}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{la}}(U,\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{\mathfrak{n}}
.

For a general representative
w\in W^{P}
of minimal length
\ell(w)
, we note that the set
\Phi_{w}:=\Phi^{+}\cap w(\Phi^{-})
has length
\ell(w)
. Choosing an appropriate ordering of the elements
\mathfrak{r}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{r}_{\ell(w)}\in\Phi_{w}
, we have a factorization into (not necessarily simple) reflections
w=s_{\mathfrak{r}_{1}}\dots s_{\mathfrak{r}_{\ell}}
. Note that since
w
is minimal length, for all
i
we have the associated nilpotent element
X_{\mathfrak{r}_{i}}\in\mathfrak{u}
lies in the nilpotent radical of the Siegel parabolic. We likewise set
w^{\prime}:=s_{\mathfrak{r}_{1}}w=s_{\mathfrak{r}_{2}}\dots s_{\mathfrak{r}_{\ell}}
. Then in fact we have that
w^{\prime}(\Phi^{+})=(w(\Phi^{+})\setminus\{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}\})\cup\{\mathfrak{r}_{1}\}
. Now let
\mathfrak{n}_{w^{\prime}}:=w\mathfrak{n}w^{-1}\cap w^{\prime}\mathfrak{n}(w^{\prime})^{-1}\subseteq w\mathfrak{n}w^{-1}
be the subalgebra generated by the roots in
(w(\Phi^{+})\setminus\{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}\})
. Then in fact
\dim\mathfrak{n}_{w^{\prime}}=\dim\mathfrak{n}-1
, as if it generated the entire Lie algebra
w\mathfrak{n}w^{-1}
, then the nilpotent Lie algebra
w^{\prime}\mathfrak{n}(w^{\prime})^{-1}
would contain both
X_{\mathfrak{r}_{1}},X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}}
, contradicting nilpotence. Moreover,
\mathfrak{n}_{w^{\prime}}
is in fact a Lie ideal, since
[X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}},X_{\mathfrak{s}}]\in X_{\mathfrak{t}}
for some
\mathfrak{t}\in w(\Phi^{+})
but it clearly cannot be
-\mathfrak{r}_{1}
. The same reasoning also shows that in fact
X_{\mathfrak{r}_{1}}
also normalizes
\mathfrak{n}_{w^{\prime}}.
The quotient
w\mathfrak{n}w^{-1}/\mathfrak{n}_{w^{\prime}}\simeq\langle X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}}\rangle
is then a
1
-dimensional nilpotent Lie algebra with representation coming from the negative root
-\mathfrak{r}_{1}
.

Now first we claim there is a short exact sequences of complexes

0\to(I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{\mathfrak{n}}\to(I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{w^{-1}\mathfrak{n}_{w^{\prime}}w}\xrightarrow{w^{-1}X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}}w}(I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{w^{-1}\mathfrak{n}_{w^{\prime}}w}\to 0.

(23)

The only claim to prove is that the final map is surjective, which is equivalent to showing
(I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{w^{-1}\mathfrak{n}_{w^{\prime}}w}
is an
R\Gamma_{\langle w^{-1}X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}}w\rangle}
-acyclic object, where
\langle w^{-1}X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}}w\rangle
is the
1
-dimensional Lie algebra generated by
w^{-1}X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}}w
. But by the Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence, we have an embedding
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\langle w^{-1}X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}}w\rangle,(I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{w^{-1}\mathfrak{n}_{w^{\prime}}w})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathfrak{n},I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})
, and
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathfrak{n},I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})=0
by
Corollary

3.9
, which gives the claim.

Passing to the long exact sequence associated to (
23
) and passing to cokernels/kernels gives the sequence

0\to\mathrm{H}^{i-1}((I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{w^{-1}\mathfrak{n}_{w^{\prime}}w})_{w^{-1}X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}}w}\otimes(\langle w^{-1}X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}}w\rangle)^{\ast}\to\mathrm{H}^{i}((I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{\mathfrak{n}})\to\mathrm{H}^{i}((I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{w^{-1}\mathfrak{n}_{w^{\prime}}w})^{w^{-1}X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}}w}\to 0.

Precomposing with the natural quotient map gives us a map

\mathrm{H}^{i-1}((I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{w^{-1}\mathfrak{n}_{w^{\prime}}w})\otimes(\langle w^{-1}X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}}w\rangle)^{\ast}\to\mathrm{H}^{i}((I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{\mathfrak{n}})
. The inclusion
(I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{w^{-1}w^{\prime}\mathfrak{n}(w^{\prime})^{-1}w}\hookrightarrow(I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{w^{-1}\mathfrak{n}_{w^{\prime}}w}
then gives a map

c_{\mathfrak{r}_{1}}:\mathrm{H}^{i-1}((I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{w^{-1}w^{\prime}\mathfrak{n}(w^{\prime})^{-1}w})\otimes\langle X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}}\rangle^{\ast}\to\mathrm{H}^{i}((I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{\mathfrak{n}}).

(24)

Combining similar sequences for the invariance of
w^{-1}w^{\prime}\mathfrak{n}(w^{-1}w^{\prime})^{-1}
, and iterating for all
\mathfrak{r}_{i}
, we get a natural map

s:\mathrm{H}^{i-\ell(w)}((I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{w^{-1}\mathfrak{n}w})\otimes\bigotimes_{i}(w^{-1}X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{i}}w)^{\ast}\xrightarrow{s}\mathrm{H}^{i}((I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{\mathfrak{n}}),

which is defined by a formula
s:=c_{\mathfrak{r}_{\ell(w)}}\circ\dots\circ c_{\mathfrak{r}_{1}}
.
Now we first note that as a
T
-module,
\bigotimes_{i}(w^{-1}X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{i}}w)^{\ast}\simeq\mathrm{H}^{\ell(w)}(w^{-1}\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}}w,\mathbf{1})\simeq\tau_{w}^{-1}\mathrm{H}^{\ell(w)}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1})^{N_{w,\overline{U}}}\simeq L(\chi_{w})
. Moreover, by the example in the
w=1
case we have a natural map
\iota:\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{n}}\hookrightarrow(I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{w^{-1}\mathfrak{n}w}
given by mapping a vector
v
to the associated constant function. On cohomology we then get a map

\iota_{\ast}:\tau_{w}^{-1}\mathrm{H}^{i-\ell(w)}(\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{n}})\otimes L(\chi_{w})\to\tau_{w}^{-1}\mathrm{H}^{i-\ell(w)}((I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{w^{-1}\mathfrak{n}w})\otimes L(\chi_{w})
, which upon composing with
s
gives a
T
-equivariant endomorphism
\operatorname{CT}_{w}\circ s\circ\iota_{\ast}:\tau_{w}^{-1}\mathrm{H}^{i-\ell(w)}(\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{n}})\otimes L(\chi_{w})\to\tau_{w}^{-1}\mathrm{H}^{i-\ell(w)}(\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{n}})\otimes L(\chi_{w})
.
Note in fact that the map
\tau_{w}^{-1}\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{n}}\to I_{w}(\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{w^{-1}\mathfrak{n}w}
factors through
(I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{w^{-1}Uw,w^{-1}\mathfrak{n}_{n}w}
. After taking
N_{n,0}
invariants we thus also have an injection
\iota_{0}\tau_{w}^{-1}\Pi^{\operatorname{la},N_{n,0}}\hookrightarrow(I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{w^{-1}N_{0}w}
. Likewise, taking (
w^{\prime}
-twisted for varying
w^{\prime}
)
N_{0}
-invariants of (
24
) and its invariants for all
i
yields a map

s_{0}\circ\iota_{0,\ast}:\mathrm{H}^{i-\ell(w)}(\tau_{w}^{-1}\Pi^{\operatorname{la},N_{n,0}}\otimes_{L}L(\chi_{w}))\to\mathrm{H}^{i}((I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{N_{0}}),

such that the endomorphism
\operatorname{CT}_{w}\circ s_{0}\circ\iota_{\ast,0}:\mathrm{H}^{i-\ell(w)}(\tau_{w}^{-1}\Pi^{\operatorname{la},N_{n,0}}\otimes_{L}L(\chi_{w}))\to\mathrm{H}^{i-\ell(w)}(\tau_{w}^{-1}\Pi^{\operatorname{la},N_{n,0}}\otimes_{L}L(\chi_{w}))
is merely the map induced from
\operatorname{CT}_{w}\circ s\circ\iota_{\ast}
by passing to
N_{n,0}
-invariants. The proposition then boils down to the following lemma.

Lemma 4.7
.

The composition

\operatorname{CT}_{w}\circ s\circ\iota_{\ast}:\mathrm{H}^{i-\ell(w)}(\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{n}}(\chi_{w}))\xrightarrow{\iota_{\ast}}\mathrm{H}^{i-\ell(w)}((I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{w^{-1}\mathfrak{n}w}(\chi_{w}))\xrightarrow{s}\mathrm{H}^{i}(I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})^{\mathfrak{n}})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{CT}_{w}}\mathrm{H}^{i-\ell(w)}(\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{n}}(\chi_{w}))

is the identity map.

Proof.

We pass to the associated (total) Chevalley–Eilenberg complexes, and use the explicit description of
\operatorname{CT}_{w}
from (
22
). In particular, we need to explicitly describe

s:\mathrm{H}^{i-\ell(w)}(\operatorname{Tot}(\operatorname{CE}(w^{-1}\mathfrak{n}w,I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})))\otimes\bigotimes_{i=1}^{\ell(w)}\langle w^{-1}X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{i}}w\rangle^{\ast}\to\mathrm{H}^{i}(\operatorname{Tot}(\operatorname{CE}(\mathfrak{n},I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}))).

First, note the map
\iota_{\ast}:\mathrm{H}^{i-\ell(w)}(\operatorname{Tot}(\operatorname{CE}(\mathfrak{n}_{n},\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}))\to\mathrm{H}^{i-\ell(w)}(\operatorname{Tot}(\operatorname{CE}(w^{-1}\mathfrak{n}w,I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})))
sends a cocycle
\sum Y_{A}^{\vee}\otimes f_{A}\mapsto\sum w^{-1}(Y_{A}^{\vee})\otimes\iota(f_{A})
. Now we first look at the map
c_{\mathfrak{r}_{1}}:\mathrm{H}^{i-1}(\operatorname{Tot}(\bigwedge(w^{-1}w^{\prime}\mathfrak{n}w(w^{\prime})^{-1}w)^{\ast}\otimes I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}))\otimes\langle w^{-1}X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}}w\rangle\to\mathrm{H}^{i}(\operatorname{Tot}(\operatorname{CE}(\mathfrak{n},I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})))
. Indeed,
c_{\mathfrak{r}_{1}}
arises from an edge/connecting map
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\langle w^{-1}X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}}w\rangle,\mathrm{H}^{i-1}(\operatorname{Tot}(\operatorname{CE}(w^{-1}\mathfrak{n}_{w^{\prime}}w,I_{w}\Pi))))\to\mathrm{H}^{i}(\operatorname{Tot}(\operatorname{CE}(\mathfrak{n},I_{w}\Pi^{\operatorname{la}})))
of a Hochschild-Serre type spectral sequence, so is naturally an inflation map which in terms of explicit cocycles and using the Chevalley–Eilenberg complex for
\langle w^{-1}X_{1}w\rangle
sends the class of

[\sum w^{-1}(X_{-\mathfrak{r}_{1}})\otimes w^{-1}(X_{A})\otimes w^{-1}(Y_{B}^{\vee})\wedge w^{-1}(Z_{C}^{\vee})\otimes f_{B,C}]\mapsto[\sum w^{-1}(X_{-r_{1}}^{\vee})\wedge w^{-1}(X_{A}^{\vee})\wedge w^{-1}(Y_{B}^{\vee})\wedge w^{-1}(Z_{C}^{\vee})\otimes f].

Iterating this description for the other
s_{\mathfrak{r}_{i}}
, we see that the map
s=s_{\mathfrak{r}_{\ell(w)}}\circ\dots\circ s_{\mathfrak{r}_{1}}

explicitly sends a cocycle

[\sum w^{-1}(Y_{B}^{\vee})\wedge w^{-1}(Z_{C}^{\vee})\otimes f_{A,B,C}]\mapsto[\sum w^{-1}(X_{[\ell(w)]}^{\vee})\wedge w^{-1}(Y_{B}^{\vee})\wedge w^{-1}(Z_{C}^{\vee})\otimes f_{B,C}].

(25)

Altogether, the map
s\circ\iota_{\ast}
sends

[\sum Y_{B}^{\vee}\otimes f_{B}]\mapsto[\sum w^{-1}(X_{[\ell(w)]}^{\vee})\wedge w^{-1}(Y_{B}^{\vee})\otimes\iota(f_{B})].

For the purposes of proving that
s\circ\iota_{\ast}
is a section, we may assume that we start with
v_{0}=f_{\emptyset}\otimes w^{-1}(X_{[\ell(w)]}^{\vee}
for
f_{\emptyset}\in\Pi^{i-\ell(w),\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{n}}\subset\operatorname{Tot}^{i-\ell(w)}(\operatorname{CE}(\mathfrak{n}_{n},\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}))
, since
\Pi^{\operatorname{la}}
is a complex of
R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{n}_{n}}
-acyclic objects. Then
(s\circ\iota_{\ast})([v_{0}])=[w^{-1}X_{[\ell(w)]}^{\vee}\otimes\iota(f_{\emptyset})]

On the other hand, from (
22
) the composition of
f\mapsto f(w)
, together with the projection map

\overline{E}_{\ell(w)}:\mathrm{H}^{i}(\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{w}})\to\mathrm{H}^{i-\ell(w)}(\tau_{w}^{-1}\Pi^{\operatorname{la},\mathfrak{n}_{n}}\otimes_{L}L(\chi_{w}))

sends

[\sum w^{-1}(Y_{B}^{\vee})\wedge w^{-1}(X_{[\ell(w)]}^{\vee})\otimes f]\mapsto[\sum w^{-1}(Y_{B}^{\vee})\otimes f(w)\otimes w^{-1}(X_{[1,\dots,\ell(w)]}^{\vee})],

Then the composition with
s\circ\iota_{\ast}
sends
[v_{0}]\mapsto[\iota(f_{\emptyset})(w)\otimes w^{-1}X_{[\ell(w)]}^{\vee}]=[f_{\emptyset}\otimes w^{-1}X_{[\ell(w)]}^{\vee}]=[v_{0}]
which is indeed the identity.

∎

This lemma finishes the proof of the first part of the proposition, and by our discussion above, implies the second part of the proposition by passing to
N_{n,0}
-invariants.

∎

Remark 4.8
.

One might wonder to what extent this argument could be generalized to give surjectivity on the other terms
\mathrm{H}^{i-j}(R\Gamma_{\mathfrak{n}_{n}}(\Pi\otimes\mathrm{H}^{j}(\mathfrak{n}_{w,\overline{U}},\mathbf{1})))
for
j<\ell(w)
. The proof above certainly fails, as there is no clear analogue for this section
s
we construct.

More unfortunately, we do not expect such a surjectivity in general. Rather, we expect that the constant term must satisfy an algebraicity condition with respect to the
\mathfrak{t}
-action (Roughly speaking, the
N_{0}
action on functions on some
PwN
intertwines positive and negative roots, which makes torus elements appear). Thus, one should only expect the “maximal” degree shift to extend over the whole weight space.

4.9
Degree shifting maps

We can now combine our work with previous sections to realize all possible degrees of Jacquet functors for
G=\operatorname{GL}_{n}/F
in the middle degree Jacquet functors of
\widetilde{G}
.

Theorem 4.10
.

Let
\widetilde{K}^{p}\subset\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+}}^{p,\infty})
be a good subgroup which is decomposed with respect to
\overline{P}
. Let
\mathfrak{m}\subset{\mathbf{T}}_{G}^{S}(K^{p})
be a decomposed generic, non-Eisenstein maximal ideal, and set
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}:=\mathcal{S}^{\ast}(\mathfrak{m})

Then for all
i
there exists
w_{i}\in W^{\overline{P}}
such that
J_{B}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la}})
and
\mathrm{H}^{d}(J_{B}\Pi_{c}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la}})
admits
\mathrm{H}^{i}(J_{B}(\tau_{w_{i}}^{-1}(\Pi(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\operatorname{la}})))\otimes_{L}L(\chi_{w_{i}})
as a subquotient essentially admissible representation of
T(F_{p}^{+})
.

Proof.

Note that
\mathrm{H}^{i}(J_{B}(\Pi_{\mathfrak{m}}(K^{p})^{\operatorname{la}}))=0
if
i\notin[0,\dim X_{K^{p}K_{p}}^{G}]=[0,d-1]
. Thus we may assume
i\leq d-1
.

Recall by
Lemma

2.24
and
Proposition

2.36
, we have
\mathrm{H}^{d}(J_{B}(\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\widetilde{G}_{0}}\Pi(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\operatorname{la}}))
is a quotient of
J_{B}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p},L)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la}})
, and
\mathrm{H}^{d-1}(J_{B}(\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{P}_{0}}^{\widetilde{G}_{0}}(\Pi_{\overline{P}}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la}}))
is a subspace of
\mathrm{H}^{d}(J_{B}(\Pi_{c}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la}}))
. We now present the rest of the proof for compactly supported cohomology, with the only difference being the choice of
w_{i}
(in particular, the value of
\ell(w_{i})
).

Choose
w_{i}
such that
\ell(w_{i})=[F^{+}:{\mathbf{Q}}]n^{2}-1-i
, so that
d-\ell(w_{i})=i
. Applying
Proposition

4.6
for
w=w_{i}
,
\Pi=\Pi_{\overline{P}}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
and
\Pi_{G}=\Pi(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}
yields a continuous
T(F_{p}^{+})^{+}
-equivariant surjection
\mathrm{H}^{d-1}((I_{w_{i}}\Pi_{\overline{P}}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la}})^{N_{0}})\to\mathrm{H}^{i}(\tau_{w_{i}}^{-1}(\Pi(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\operatorname{la}})^{N_{n,0}})\otimes_{L}L(\chi_{w_{i}})
. By
Lemma

3.11
and the previous paragraph, it suffices check that this surjection remains a surjection after passing to finite slope parts.

Recalling
[
Fu22b
, Proposition 4.9]
, Jacquet functors may be computed as the finite slope part of the Hausdorff quotient of
N_{0}
-cohomology. In particular, passing to Hausdorff quotients preserves surjectivity, so we get a surjection of
T(F_{p}^{+})^{+}
-modules
\mathrm{H}_{\operatorname{haus}}^{d}((I_{w_{i}}\Pi_{\overline{P}}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la}})^{N_{0}})\to\mathrm{H}_{\operatorname{haus}}^{i}(\tau_{w_{i}}^{-1}(\Pi(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\operatorname{la}})^{N_{n,0}})\otimes_{L}L(\chi_{w_{i}})
. Now recall by the definition of
W^{\overline{P}}
we have
w_{i}N_{0}w_{i}^{-1}\supset N_{n,0}
, which implies
w_{i}T(F_{p}^{+})^{+}w_{i}^{-1}\subset T_{n}(F_{p})^{+}
, and in fact
w_{i}T(F_{p}^{+})^{++}w_{i}^{-1}\subset T_{n}(F_{p})^{++}
. Choosing an element
z\in T(F_{p}^{+})^{++}
, we thus have by
[
Fu22b
, Theorem 4.5]

a surjection
\mathrm{H}_{\operatorname{haus}}^{d}((I_{w_{i}}\Pi_{\overline{P}}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la}})_{z-\operatorname{fs}}^{N_{0}})\to\tau_{w_{i}}^{-1}(\mathrm{H}_{\operatorname{haus}}^{i}((\Pi(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\operatorname{la}})^{N_{n,0}})_{w_{i}zw_{i}^{-1}-\operatorname{fs}})\otimes_{L}L(\chi_{w_{i}})
, which in turn is a surjection

\mathrm{H}^{d}(J_{B}(I_{w_{i}}\Pi(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\operatorname{la}}))\twoheadrightarrow\tau_{w_{i}}^{-1}\mathrm{H}^{i}(J_{B_{n}}(\Pi(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\operatorname{la}}))\otimes_{L}L(\chi_{w_{i}})
, as desired.
∎

Consider the map

j_{w}:\widehat{T(F_{p}^{+})}\to\widehat{T(F_{p}^{+})}
sending a character
c
to
w^{-1}(c)\cdot\chi_{w}
. Concretely, on
\mathcal{W}
this map sends a weight
\lambda\mapsto j_{w}(\lambda)=w^{-1}(\lambda-w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}\rho^{\widetilde{G}})+w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}\rho^{\widetilde{G}}
, where
\rho^{\widetilde{G}}
is the half sum of positive roots.

Theorem 4.11
.

For any
i\in[0,d-1]
let
w_{i}\in W^{\overline{P}}
be as in
Theorem

4.10
. Then the map
\mathcal{S}^{\ast}\times j_{w_{i}}:\operatorname{Spec}{\mathbf{T}}^{S}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}\times\widehat{T(F_{p}^{+})}\to\operatorname{Spec}\widetilde{{\mathbf{T}}}^{S}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\times\widehat{T(F_{p}^{+})}
induces a closed embedding of rigid varieties
\mathcal{E}^{i}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{c}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
fitting into the following diagram

\hbox to137.03pt{\vbox to52.14pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 68.51271pt\lower-26.06998pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
