{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-13.31526pt}{-14.94737pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -18.42 -20.68)} \pgfsys@hbox{62}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}:=(\mathrm{H}^{d}(J_{B}(\Pi_{c}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la},N_{0}})))^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{i}:=\mathrm{H}^{i}(J_{B_{n}}(\Pi(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\operatorname{la}}))
be the strong duals, which are coadmissible
\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{T}}(\widehat{T})
-modules. Recall the eigenvarieties
\mathcal{E}_{c}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{P})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
and
\mathcal{E}^{i}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}
are defined via the morphisms
\psi_{c}^{d}:\widetilde{{\mathbf{T}}}^{S}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\to\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{T}}}(\mathcal{M}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d})
and
\psi^{i}:\widetilde{{\mathbf{T}}}^{S}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\to\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{T}}}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{i})
, and passing to the associated
\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{T}}
-algebras
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}},\mathcal{A}
generated by the images of
\psi_{c}^{d},\psi^{i}
respectively. By
Theorem

4.10
the map
S:\widetilde{{\mathbf{T}}}^{S}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\to{\mathbf{T}}^{S}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}
then induces a surjective morphism of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{T}}(\widehat{T})
-algebras
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\to L(\chi_{w_{i}})\otimes_{L}\tau_{w_{i}}^{-1}\mathcal{A}
.
Passing to the relative spectra over
\widehat{T}
gives the desired closed embedding, where we note that the map on
\widehat{T}
is twisted via
j_{w_{i}}
to incorporate the
T
-action on
L(\chi_{w_{i}})\otimes_{L}\tau_{w_{i}}^{-1}A
.
∎

5
The unitary group in middle degree

Theorem

4.11
enables us to study eigenvarieties for
G
via a single eigenvariety for
\widetilde{G}
. The goal of this section is study
\widetilde{G}
, and in particular prove
Theorem

1.6
. For the rest of this article, we will often suppress tame level from our notation for eigenvarieties and denote
\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}:=\mathcal{E}_{c}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}},\mathcal{E}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}:=\mathcal{E}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
.

The proof is largely inspired by the treatment given in
[
BHS17
, Section 3]
, which combines locally analytic representations with standard eigenvariety tools from
[
Buz07
]
. We note, however, the key input into their discussion is a Banach representation
\Pi
such that
\Pi|_{H}\simeq\mathcal{C}(H,L)^{\oplus M}
for some compact open subgroup
H\subset\widetilde{G}_{0}=\widetilde{G}({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{p}^{+}})\simeq\operatorname{GL}_{2n}(\mathcal{O}_{F,p})
(in other words,
\Pi
is an
injective
admissible representation of
\widetilde{G}_{0}
). Such a property is typically
not
satisfied by the (compactly supported) completed cohomology groups
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{(c)}^{i}(\widetilde{K}^{p},L)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
whenever
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}
is Eisenstein. The key fact we will need is that under a genericity assumption on
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}
, the complex
\Pi_{c}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
can be represented by a finite complex of Banach representations
\Pi_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{bdd}}
such that
\Pi_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{bdd},i}|_{H}\simeq\mathcal{C}(H,L)^{\oplus m_{i}}
for all
i
,
concentrated in degrees

[0,d]
. This property gives us good control on derived Jacquet modules in middle degree, and allows us to push through the arguments of
[
BHS17
]
.

5.1
Compactly supported cohomology

We first recall some key definitions and properties about torsion and completed cohomology in our setting.

Definition 5.2
.

A continuous representation
\overline{\rho}:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to\operatorname{GL}_{d}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
is called
decomposed generic
if there exists a prime
\ell
which splits completely in
F
, and for all places
\nu\mid\ell
of
F
,
\overline{\rho}|_{\operatorname{Gal}_{F_{\nu}}}
is unramified with the eigenvalues
\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{d}
of
\overline{\rho}(\operatorname{Frob}_{\nu})
satisfying
\alpha_{i}/\alpha_{j}\neq\ell
for all
i\neq j
.

Theorem 5.3

(Caraiani–Scholze
[
CS19
]
, Koshikawa
[
Kos21
]
)
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}
be a maximal ideal in the support of
\mathrm{H}^{\ast}(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}^{p}\widetilde{K}_{p}},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
such that
\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
is decomposed generic.
Then we have
\mathrm{H}^{i}(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}^{p}\widetilde{K}_{p}},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}=0
for
i<d
,
\mathrm{H}_{c}^{i}(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}^{p}\widetilde{K}_{p}},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}=0
for
i>d,
and
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{i}(\widetilde{K}^{p},{\mathbf{Z}}_{p})=0
for
i>d
. Consequently, the integral completed cohomology
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{i}(\widetilde{K}^{p},{\mathbf{Z}}_{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
is concentrated in degree
i=d
.

Proof.

The first part follows from
[
Kos21
, Theorem 1.3]
(which removes certain hypotheses from
[
CS19
]
), and the assertion about completed cohomology follows from
[
CS19
, Theorem 2.6.2 Lemma 4.6.2]
.
∎

In general we call
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}\subset\widetilde{{\mathbf{T}}}^{S}(\widetilde{K}^{p})

decomposed generic
if the associated Galois representation
\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
is. The same definition applies to maximal ideals
\mathfrak{m}\subset{\mathbf{T}}^{S}(K^{p})
for
G
. For the rest of this section, we will assume that
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}
is decomposed generic.

Note that dually,
Theorem

5.3
implies that
\mathrm{H}_{i}^{BM}(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}^{p}\widetilde{K}_{p}},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}=0
for
i>d
. Now consider the complex
C_{\bullet}^{\operatorname{BM},\operatorname{BS}}(\widetilde{K}^{p}\widetilde{K}_{p},{\mathcal{O}}_{L}[[\widetilde{K}_{p}]])_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
for
\widetilde{K}_{p}
pro-
p
. This is a perfect complex of
{\mathcal{O}}_{L}[|\widetilde{K}_{p}|]
-modules. We thus have
C_{\bullet}^{\operatorname{BS}}(\widetilde{K}^{p}\widetilde{K}_{p},{\mathcal{O}}_{L}[[\widetilde{K}_{p}]])_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{L}[[\widetilde{K}_{p}]]}^{\mathbb{L}}{\mathcal{O}}_{L}/\mathfrak{m}_{L}\simeq C_{\bullet}^{\operatorname{BS}}(\widetilde{K}^{p}\widetilde{K}_{p},{\mathcal{O}}_{L}[[\widetilde{K}_{p}]])_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{L}[[\widetilde{K}_{p}]]}{\mathcal{O}}_{L}/\mathfrak{m}_{L}\simeq C_{\bullet}^{\operatorname{BM},\operatorname{BS}}(\widetilde{K}^{p}\widetilde{K}_{p},{\mathcal{O}}_{L}/\mathfrak{m}_{L})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
is concentrated in degrees
[0,d]
, and so the theory of
minimal resolutions
(in the guise of
[
GN22
, Proposition 2.1.9]
) implies the following:

Proposition 5.4
.

\Pi_{c}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
is quasi-isomorphic to a complex
\Pi_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{bdd}}
of admissible Banach representations of
H\subset\widetilde{G}_{0}
concentrated in degrees
[0,d]
for such that for some pro-
p
compact open subgroup
H\subset\widetilde{G}_{0}
we have
\Pi_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\bullet,\operatorname{bdd}}\mid_{H}\simeq\mathcal{C}(H,L)^{\oplus m_{i}}
. Similarly, for
\Pi(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
is quasi-isomorphic to a complex of injective admissible representations concentrated in degrees
[d,2d]
.

Proof.

Noting that
\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{cont}}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{(c)}^{i}(\widetilde{K}^{p},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\simeq\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}_{i}^{(BM)}(\widetilde{K}^{p},{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
, it suffices to work with homology. The discussion above (in particular,
[
GN22
, Proposition 2.1.9]
) implies
C_{\bullet}^{\operatorname{BM},\operatorname{BS}}(\widetilde{K}^{p},{\mathbf{Z}}_{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
is quasi isomorphism to a complex
B_{\bullet}
of finite free
\mathcal{O}[[H]]
-modules, such that
B_{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}[[H]]]}\mathcal{O}([[H]])/\mathfrak{m}\simeq\bigoplus_{i}\mathrm{H}_{i}(\widetilde{K}^{p}H,{\mathbb{F}}_{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}[-i]
. Since
\mathrm{H}_{i}^{\operatorname{BM}}(\widetilde{K}^{p}H,{\mathbb{F}}_{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
is concentrated in degrees
[0,d]
, so is
B_{\bullet}
. Then taking
(B_{\bullet}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}}{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})^{\vee}
yields the desired complex. The statement for
\Pi(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
follows from the analogous vanishing of
\mathrm{H}_{i}(\widetilde{K}^{p},{\mathbb{F}}_{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}=0
for
i<d
.
∎

Remark 5.5
.

An alternative proof (and the one we originally conceived of) can also be given using the Poincaré duality spectral sequence for completed (co)homology
[
CE12
, 1.3]
.

5.6
Middle degree cohomology

We briefly say something about eigenvarieties
\mathcal{E}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
for middle degree completed cohomology. The direction of vanishing for torsion cohomology in fact tells us that
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{d}
coincides with Emerton’s eigenvarieties from
[
Eme06b
]
.

Lemma 5.7
.

There is an isomorphism
\mathrm{H}^{d}(J_{B}(\Pi(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la}}))\simeq J_{B}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p},L)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la}})
of essentially admissible representations of
T(F_{p}^{+})
.

Proof.

By
Proposition

5.4
we can represent
\Pi(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
by a complex of injective admissible
H
-representations
I_{d}\xrightarrow{g_{d}}\dots\xrightarrow{g_{2d-1}}I_{2d}
concentrated in degree
[d,2d]
. Thus,
\mathrm{H}^{d}(J_{B}(\Pi(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la}})
is computed by
(((\ker g_{d})^{\operatorname{la},N_{0}})_{\operatorname{haus}})_{\operatorname{fs}}
by
[
Fu22b
, Proposition 4.9]
(using that taking
N_{0}
-invariants is left exact, and thus commutes with kernels), and where
(-)_{\operatorname{haus}}
denote the Hausdorff quotient. However, since
g_{d}
is a continuous map,
(\ker g_{d})^{\operatorname{la},N_{0}}
is a closed subspace of
I_{d}^{\operatorname{la},N_{0}}
, and is thus automatically Hausdorff. Thus we actually just have
\mathrm{H}^{d}(J_{B}(\Pi(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la}}))=((\ker g_{d}^{\operatorname{la}})^{N_{0}})_{\operatorname{fs}}=J_{B}((\ker g_{d})^{\operatorname{la}})=J_{B}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p},L)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la}})
, as required.
∎

When the cohomology
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{\ast}(\widetilde{K}^{p},L)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
is concentrated in a single degree, the relation between the Jacquet functor and classical automorphic forms is also more lucid. By Emerton’s spectral sequence
[
Eme06b
, Theorem 0.5]
, concentration in one degree implies that the locally algebraic vectors
\mathrm{H}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p},L)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{lalg}}\simeq\bigoplus_{W}W^{\vee}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p},W)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}},
where
W
ranges over irreducible algebraic representations of
\widetilde{G}(F_{p}^{+})
, and
\mathrm{H}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p},W^{\vee}):=\varinjlim_{\widetilde{K}_{p}}\mathrm{H}^{d}(\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{K}^{p}\widetilde{K}_{p}},W^{\vee})
. So the locally algebraic vectors is directly related to classical cohomology, and by
[
Eme06a
, Proposition 4.3.6]

J_{B}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p},L)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{lalg}})\simeq\bigoplus_{\mathsf{L}(\lambda)}J_{B}(\mathsf{L}(\lambda)\otimes_{L}\mathrm{H}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p},\mathsf{L}(\lambda)^{\vee})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}})\simeq\bigoplus_{\lambda}\lambda\otimes_{L}J_{B}(\mathrm{H}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p},\mathsf{L}(\lambda)^{\vee}))(\delta_{B}^{-1})
, where
\lambda
ranges over highest weight vectors
\lambda\in({\mathbf{Z}}_{\operatorname{dom}}^{2n})^{\operatorname{Hom}(F^{+},\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})}
.
In general, we call a point
x=(y,\delta)\in\mathcal{E}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}

classical
if the space
\operatorname{Hom}_{T(F_{p}^{+})}(\delta,J_{B}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p},L)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{lalg}})[\widetilde{{\mathbf{T}}}^{S}=y])\neq 0
.

Now fix an isomorphism
\iota:\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}\simeq{\mathbf{C}}
and
\pi
is an automorphic representation of
\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+}})
of weight
\lambda
which is unramified outside
S
and thus has Hecke eigenvalues
\iota^{-1}\circ\psi_{\pi}:\widetilde{{\mathbf{T}}}^{S}\to\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}
.

Suppose moreover
J_{B}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p},L)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{lalg}})[\psi_{\pi}]\neq 0
, then in fact that
J_{B}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p},\mathsf{L}(\lambda)))[\psi_{\pi}]\neq 0
. Now for all
\overline{\nu}\in S_{p}(F^{+})
suppose the Jacquet module
J_{B}(\iota^{-1}\circ\pi_{\overline{\nu}})
admits a subquotient
\theta_{\overline{\nu}}:T(F_{\overline{\nu}}^{+})\to\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
and set
\theta:=\prod_{\overline{\nu}}\theta_{\overline{\nu}}:T(F_{p}^{+})\to\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
.
Then by the above discussion we have
\operatorname{Hom}_{T(F_{p}^{+})}(\lambda^{\vee}\theta\cdot\delta_{B}^{-1},J_{B}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p},L)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la}}))\neq 0
, and thus
(\psi,\lambda^{\vee}\cdot\theta\cdot\delta_{B}^{-1})\in\mathcal{E}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}(\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})
.

Definition 5.8
.

A character
\delta_{\overline{\nu}}\in\widehat{(F_{\nu}^{\times})^{2n}}(\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})
is
numerically noncritical
if for all
\tau\in\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}(F_{\nu},\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})
we have:

1.

the weights
\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(\delta_{\overline{\nu},1}),\dots\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(\delta_{\overline{\nu},2n})
are a (strictly) increasing sequence of integers;

2.

for each
i=1,\dots,2n-1
we have

v_{p}((\delta_{\overline{\nu},1}\dots\delta_{\overline{\nu},i})(p))<\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(\delta_{\nu,i+1})-\operatorname{wt}(\delta_{\overline{\nu},i}).

The motivation for this definition comes from small-slope classicality criteria.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
z=(y,\delta)\in\mathcal{E}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
be such that
j_{2n}(\delta)=\lambda\delta_{\operatorname{sm}},
where
\lambda
is a strictly dominant algebraic character,
\delta_{\operatorname{sm}}
is smooth, and
\delta
is numerically noncritical. Then
z
is classical.

Proof.

This result is a formal consequence of Emerton’s original criterion
[
Eme06a
, Theorem 4.4.5]
for when the Jacquet functor commutes with passing to locally algebraic vectors. (See
[
NT21
, Remark 2.21]
for the relationship between
j_{n}
, numerically noncritical, and non-critical in the sense of
[
Eme06a
, Definition 4.4.3]
, together with
Remark

2.6
).
∎

While this corollary gives a clear understanding of “classical points” in
\mathcal{E}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
, we remark
\mathcal{E}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
is not well-suited to proving some sort of density statement, since the coherent sheaf
\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}=(J_{B}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p},L)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la}}))^{\prime}
(locally) contains
torsion
over the weight space
\mathcal{W}
. Indeed, by
Corollary

2.31
and
Lemma

2.24
, we have an embedding of a (locally) torsion module
\mathcal{M}_{\partial,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
. So we focus instead on the compactly supported case.

5.10
Middle-degree derived Jacquet module

We use the complex
\Pi_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{bdd}}
of injective admissible Banach representations of
H
from

Proposition

5.4
which represents
\Pi_{c}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
. Since these are
finite
complexes of injectives, the usual trick of
[
Wei94
, Corollary 10.4.7]
upgrades this quasi-isomorphism to a homotopy equivalence of chain complexes in
\operatorname{Ban}_{H}^{\operatorname{ad}}
. This fact provides
H
-equivariant homotopies

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{bdd},\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

(26)

such that
s,n
are
H
-equivariant homotopy inverses, and
i,p
are
\widetilde{G}_{0}
-equivariant homotopy inverses.

Using (
26
) after choosing an element
z\in T(F_{p}^{+})^{++}
we can consider the cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{d}(J_{B}(\Pi_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{bdd},\operatorname{la}}))
, which by
Lemma

2.7
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{H}^{d}(J_{B}(\Pi_{c}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la}}))
. This module is an essentially admissible
T(F_{p}^{+})
-representation by
[
Fu22b
, Proposition 3.20]
. Let
Y_{H}\subset T(F_{p}^{+})
be the closed subgroup generated by
z
and
T(F_{p}^{+})\cap H
. Since
\Pi_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{bdd}}
is concentrated in degrees
[0,d]
, there is a natural surjection of essentially admissible
Y_{H}
-representations

J_{B}(\Pi_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d,\operatorname{bdd},\operatorname{la}})\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{d}(J_{B}(\Pi_{c}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la}})).

Let
\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d},\mathcal{N}
be the coherent sheaves on
\widehat{Y}_{H}\simeq\mathcal{W}_{H}\times_{L}{\mathbf{G}}_{m}
associated to
\mathrm{H}^{d}(J_{B}(\Pi_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{bdd},\operatorname{la}}))
and
J_{B}(\Pi_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{bdd},d,\operatorname{la}})
respectively, so that we have a natural embedding
\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{N}
. Let
\mathcal{Z}_{c}^{d}:=\operatorname{supp}_{\mathcal{W}\times_{L}{\mathbf{G}}_{m}}\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{N}}:=\operatorname{supp}_{\mathcal{W}_{H}\times_{L}{\mathbf{G}}_{m}}\mathcal{N}
be their supports. Note that the restriction map
\operatorname{res}_{H}:\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{W}_{H}
is a finite étale cover, and also that we have an inclusion of analytic closed subsets
\operatorname{res}_{H}(\mathcal{Z}_{c}^{d})\subset\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{N}}\subset\mathcal{W}_{H}\times_{L}{\mathbf{G}}_{m}
.

We can use standard methods in the theory of eigenvarieties (as presented in
[
BHS17
, §3, §5]
) to prove that in some sense,
\mathcal{M}
is “large” over the weight space
\mathcal{W}
. We note that the following arguments have strong similarities with those in
Proposition

2.18
. The main difference is that here we work with (a version of ) the “
U_{1}z
”-action instead of “
U_{2}z
”-action, which are homotopic by
Lemma

2.11
. First, let
\pi_{\mathcal{W}}:\widehat{Y}_{H}\to\mathcal{W}_{H}
denote the projection onto weight space.

Proposition 5.11
.

The closed analytic subset
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{N}}\subset\mathcal{W}_{H}\times_{L}{\mathbf{G}}_{m}
is naturally a Fredholm hypersurface.
Moreover there exists an affinoid open admissible cover
(U_{i}^{\prime\prime})_{i}\subset\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{N}}
so that the induced maps
\pi_{\mathcal{W}}:U_{i}^{\prime\prime}\to\pi_{\mathcal{W}}(U_{i}^{\prime\prime})=W_{i}^{\prime\prime}\subset\mathcal{W}_{H}
is a finite surjective onto a Zariski open
W_{i}^{\prime\prime}\subset\mathcal{W}_{H}
, and
U_{i}^{\prime\prime}
is a connected component of
\pi_{\mathcal{W}}^{-1}(W_{i}^{\prime\prime})
, over which
\Gamma(U_{i}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{N})
is a finite projective
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}_{H}}(W_{i}^{\prime\prime})
-module.

Correspondingly, there is a admissible affinoid cover
(U_{i}^{\prime})_{i}\subset\mathcal{Z}_{c}^{d}
such that the induced maps
\pi_{\mathcal{W}}:U_{i}^{\prime}\to W_{i}^{\prime}:=\pi_{\mathcal{W}}(U_{i})\subset\mathcal{W}
are finite surjective onto an affinoid open, and
U_{i}^{\prime}
is a connected component of
\pi_{\mathcal{W}}^{-1}(W_{i}^{\prime})
. Moreover,
\Gamma(U_{i}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d})
finitely generated torsion-free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}(W_{i}^{\prime})
-module, and
\operatorname{res}_{H}(\mathcal{Z}_{c}^{d})
is a union of irreducible components of
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{N}}
.

Proof.

The proof follows the arguments of
[
BHS17
, Lemme 3.10, Proposition 5.3]
up to replacing certain inputs. We give a summary of the main steps, and how they push through in our setting. Let
V:=\Pi_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{bdd},d,\operatorname{la}}
.
Suppose that
H\subset\widetilde{G}_{0}
is uniform pro-
p
with an Iwahori decomposition
H\simeq(\overline{N}\cap H)\times(T\cap H)\times(N\cap H)
. Let
N_{H}:=N\cap H
. By construction we have that
V|_{H}\simeq\mathcal{C}(H,L)^{\oplus m_{d}}
for some
m_{d}\geq 0
. For the statements regarding
\mathcal{N}
, the essential key point is
[
BHS17
, Proposition 5.3]
in our setting:
that is, there exists a countable admissible affinoid open cover
W_{1}^{\prime}\subset\dots\subset W_{h}^{\prime}\subset\dots
of
\mathcal{W}
and (Pr) Banach
A_{h}:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}(W_{h}^{\prime})
-modules
V_{h}
such that
((V^{\operatorname{la}})^{N_{0}})^{\prime}\simeq\varprojlim_{h}V_{h}
, and the dual
U_{z}
-action induces
A_{h}
-compact endomorphisms
z_{h}:V_{h}\to V_{h}
fitting into a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{h+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{h+1}\otimes_{A_{h+1}}A_{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{h+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{h+1}\otimes_{A_{h+1}}A_{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{h+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{h+1}\otimes_{A_{h+1}}1_{A_{h}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{h}}

where the
\beta_{h}
are
A_{h}
-compact.
In our case, it suffices to show such a description goes through when one only has this action up to homotopy, namely
U_{z}
as defined via (
26
). The key will be that the
V_{h}
are (roughly) functorial in
V
, and the morphisms
\alpha_{h}
comes from some property of increasing a radius of analyticity, which is also functorial.

More rigorously, just as in
Proposition

2.18
we use the functors
(-)_{H}^{(h)}
constructed in
[
CD14
]
. First note the Iwahori decomposition induces a topological isomorphism

\mathcal{C}^{(h)}(H,L)\simeq\mathcal{C}^{(h)}(N\cap H,L)\widehat{\otimes}_{L}\mathcal{C}^{(h)}(T\cap H,L)\widehat{\otimes}_{L}\mathcal{C}^{(h)}(N\cap H,L).

A version of Emerton’s untwisting lemma
[
Eme17
, 3.6.4]
readily implies that we have a
T_{H}
-equivariant isomorphism

V^{N_{0}}|_{H}\simeq\mathcal{C}^{(h)}(H,L)^{\oplus m_{d},N_{0}}\simeq\mathcal{C}^{(h)}(\overline{N}\cap H,L)^{\oplus m_{d}}\widehat{\otimes}_{L}\mathcal{C}^{(h)}(T_{H},L).

(27)

Now setting
B_{h}:=D_{p^{-r_{h}}}(T_{H},L)
, where
r_{h}=\frac{1}{\varphi(p^{h})}=\frac{1}{(p-1)p^{h-1}}
. The natural restriction map
B_{h+1}\to B_{h}
necessarily factors through
D_{<p^{-r_{h}}}(T\cap H,L)\simeq D^{(h)}(T_{H},L):=(C^{(h)}(T\cap H,L))^{\prime}
. Now set

\displaystyle V_{h}

\displaystyle:=(\mathcal{C}^{(h)}(\overline{N}\cap H,L)^{\oplus m})^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}_{L}B_{h}

\displaystyle\simeq(\mathcal{C}^{(h)}(\overline{N}\cap H,L)^{m}\widehat{\otimes}_{L}\mathcal{C}^{(h+1)}(T\cap H,L))^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}_{D^{(h)}(T_{H},L)}B_{h}.

By (
27
) and the fact that
\varinjlim_{h}(V_{H}^{h})^{N_{H}}=V^{N_{H}}
we readily have
(V^{N_{h}})^{\prime}\simeq\varprojlim_{h}V_{h}
. By definition, the
V_{H}
are ONable Banach modules over
B_{h}
. To recover a weight space description, note that
T_{H}\simeq{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}^{\oplus M}
for some
M\geq 0
, so the Amice transform implies that
B_{h}\simeq\Gamma(W_{h}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}_{H}})
for some affinoid open
W_{h}^{\prime\prime}\subset\mathcal{W}_{H}
, and the collection of
W_{h}^{\prime\prime}
form an admissible cover.

We now show the dual Hecke action
U_{z}^{\ast}
both descends to the
V_{h}
and has the relevant factorization properties in
Section

5.10
. Shrinking
H
if necessary, we may assume both
H
and
zHz^{-1}
act on
\Pi_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{bdd}}
. Then we claim that if
v\in V_{H}^{(h)}
, then
U_{z}v\in V_{zHz^{-1}}^{(h)}
. Indeed, this fact would be true if
V
had an action of the full group
\widetilde{G}
, rather than an action up to homotopy. However, note that the homotopies
s,p,i,n
are each continuous and
\widetilde{G}_{0}
-equivariant, and
()_{H}^{(h)}
,
()_{zHz^{-1}}^{(h)}
are both functors, so the result follows from the corresponding fact on the cochain complex of Banach representations
A^{\bullet}
. This simple fact is all we need to push through
[
BHS17
, Proposition 5.3]
.

Namely, this fact along with
Section

2.16
implies the Hecke action satisfies

U_{z}(\mathcal{C}^{(h)}(\overline{N}\cap H,L)^{m}\widehat{\otimes}_{L}\mathcal{C}^{(h)}(T\cap H,L))\subset\mathcal{C}^{(h-1)}(\overline{N}\cap H,L)^{m}\widehat{\otimes}_{L}\mathcal{C}^{(h)}(T\cap H,L).

(28)

We then get the diagram
Section

5.10
by setting
z_{h}:=U_{z}^{\ast}
induced on the first factor (which can be defined since all the homotopies and the group action on
A^{\bullet}
are continuous), and
\alpha_{h}:=\alpha_{h}^{\prime}\otimes 1
where
\alpha_{h}^{\prime}
is dual to the Hecke action map (
28
), and

\beta_{h}=\beta_{h}^{\prime}\otimes 1
where
\beta_{h}^{\prime}
is simply the dual map of the natural inclusion

\mathcal{C}^{(h)}(\overline{N}\cap H,L)\widehat{\otimes}_{L}\mathcal{C}^{(h+1)}(T\cap H,L)\to\mathcal{C}^{(h+1)}(\overline{N}\cap H,L)\widehat{\otimes}_{L}\mathcal{C}^{(h+1)}(T\cap H,L).

It is a general fact that the induced map
\beta_{h}^{\prime}
is compact, and so
\beta_{h}^{\prime}
is a
B_{h}
-compact map. The rest of the Proposition regarding
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{N}}
then follows exactly as in
[
BHS17
, Lemme 3.10]
.

We now turn to
\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d}
. First note that for an admissible cover
(U_{h}^{\prime\prime})_{h}
of
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{N}}
, we have embeddings of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}_{H}}(W_{h}^{\prime})
-modules
\Gamma(U_{h}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d})\hookrightarrow\Gamma(U_{h}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{N})
, so that
\Gamma(U_{h}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d})
is a submodule of a finite projective module over a noetherian integral domain, and is thus finitely generated and torsion-free over
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}_{H}}(W_{h}^{\prime\prime})
. We also have an inclusion
\operatorname{res}_{H}(\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d}})\subset\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{N}}
. In particular, either
\operatorname{supp}_{W_{h}^{\prime\prime}}\Gamma(U_{h}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d})=\emptyset
or
W_{h}^{\prime\prime}
. As locally on
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{N}}
, the projection to
\mathcal{W}_{H}
is finite, it follows that
\dim\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d}}=\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{N}}
, and thus
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d}}
is a union of components of
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{N}}
, and is thus also a Fredholm hypersurface.

To get the statement over the whole
\mathcal{W}
, we again utilize the finite étale cover
\operatorname{res}_{H}:\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{W}_{H}
. That is, we define the admissible cover
U_{h}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d}}
by
U_{h}^{\prime}:=(\operatorname{res}_{H}\times\operatorname{id}_{{\mathbf{G}}_{m}})^{-1}(U_{h}^{\prime\prime})
. Moreover, if
W_{h}^{\prime\prime}
is an admissible cover of
\mathcal{W}_{H}
admitting diagrams as in (
5.10
), then set
A_{h}:=L[T_{0}]\otimes_{L[T\cap H]\cap H]}B_{h}\simeq\Gamma(W_{h}^{\prime},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}})
for
W_{h}^{\prime}=\operatorname{res}_{H}^{-1}(W_{h}^{\prime\prime})
.
Moreover, since
A_{h}
is a finite étale
B_{h}
-algebra,
\Gamma(U_{h}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d})
’s is a direct factor of
L[T_{0}]\otimes_{L[T\cap H]}\Gamma(U_{h}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d})
, and thus a submodule of a finite free
A_{h}
-module.
∎

Proposition 5.12
.

There exists an admissible affinoid open cover
(U_{i})_{i\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}}
of
\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
such that
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{c}^{d}}(U_{i})
is a subalgebra of the endomorphism of a finitely generated torsion-free algebra over
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}(\kappa(U_{i}))
.

As a result, the eigenvariety
\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
is equidimensional of dimension
\dim\mathcal{W}
with no embedded components. Moreover, the image of any irreducible component of
\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
along the weight map
\kappa:\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}\to\mathcal{W}
is Zariski open.

Proof.

This proof is also essentially an adaptation of
[
BHS17
, Proposition 3.11, Corollaire 3.12, Corollaire 3.13]
to the setting at hand. The only difference is that instead locally for an admissible open
U_{i}
, we have an embedding
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}}(U_{i})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}(W_{i})}(\Gamma(U_{i}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d}))
, where
\Gamma(U_{i}^{\prime},\mathcal{N}_{c}^{d})
is torsion–free over
A:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}(W_{i})
by
Proposition

5.11
. As a finite
A
-module,
\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}(W_{i})}(\Gamma(U_{i}^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d}))
is torsion-free itself. To prove
\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
is equidimensional it suffices to work affine locally and prove
B:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{c}^{d}}(U_{i})
is equidimensional. Let
\mathfrak{p}\subset B
be any associated prime. Then we have an embedding of
B
-modules
B/\mathfrak{p}\hookrightarrow B
, which implies
B/\mathfrak{p}
must also be
A
-torsion-free. In particular, the structure morphism
A\to B/\mathfrak{p}
is injective, and is thus an integral extension of Noetherian rings. Standard dimension theory then implies
\dim(B/\mathfrak{p})=\dim A=\dim\mathcal{W}
. Since this equality holds for any
minimal
prime, we have
B
is equidimensional. Moreover, since this dimension is constant for any associated prime, the only associated primes are minimal primes, and thus there are no embedded components.

Fixing an irreducible component
C\subset\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
, its image in
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d}}
is then irreducible of dimension
\dim\mathcal{W}=\dim Z_{\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d}}
, and is thus also irreducible. An irreducible component of a Fredholm hypersurface is also a Fredholm hypersurface by
[
Con99
, 4.2.2]
, and the openness is then proven in
[
Che04
, 6.4.4]

∎

We now can prove our abstract density criterion. Let
f:\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}\to\widehat{T}\to\mathcal{W}\times{\mathbf{G}}_{m}
, be the structure map. Note that by definition
f
factors through
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d}}\subset\mathcal{W}\times{\mathbf{G}}_{m}
.

Proposition 5.13
.

If
S\subsetneq\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d}}
and
Y\subsetneq\mathcal{W}
are closed subvarieties such that each irreducible component is positive codimension, then the set

\mathcal{E}_{S,Y}=\{e\in\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}:f(e)\notin S,\kappa(e)\notin Y,e\text{ locally dominant algebraic},j_{2n}(e)\text{ numerically noncritical}\}

is Zariski dense in
\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
, and accumulates at any point of algebraic weight.

Proof.

The proof falls into two steps:

1.

The weights
w(E)
accumulates at any algebraic point of
\mathcal{W}
;

2.

Use the first point to show Zariski-density of
E
.

Before proving Step 1, we show Step 1 implies Step 2 (i.e. Zariski-density). It suffices to show any irreducible component
X\subset\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
lies in the closure. By
Proposition

5.12
,
\kappa(X)\subset\mathcal{W}
is Zariski open, and thus there is a point
x\in X
of algebraic weight. Now suppose
\overline{\mathcal{E}_{S,Y}}\not\supset X
. Then
\dim\overline{\mathcal{E}_{S,Y}\cap X}<\dim X=\dim\mathcal{W}
. But recall that locally on the eigenvariety
\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
we have an affinoid open
U\ni x
such that the induced map
\kappa_{U\cap X}:U\cap X\to\kappa(U\cap X)
is finite surjective.
As
\kappa_{U\cap X}
is finite, the image of
\overline{\mathcal{E}_{S,Y}\cap U\cap X}
is an analytic closed subset of
\kappa(U\cap X)
containing
\kappa(\mathcal{E}_{S,Y}\cap U\cap X)
which by the accumulation property is both nonempty and equal to
\kappa_{X}(U)
, which has
\dim\mathcal{W}
, contradicting the lack of density up top.

Now we prove Step 1: Let
x
be a point of algebraic weight. Consider a connected affinoid open neighborhood
X\ni U\ni x
contained in a fixed irreducible component
X\subset\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
, such that
\kappa:U\to\kappa(U)
is finite surjective and
V:=\kappa(U)\subset\mathcal{W}
is itself affinoid open. As
S
is a nowhere dense closed subset of
X
, we have
S\cap U\subset U
itself is an affinoid which is a union of irreducible proper closed subsets
Z_{i_{1}},\dots,Z_{i_{k}}
.

Now following
[
BHS17
, Proposition 3.19]
, on the affinoid open
U
, the function
(y,\delta)\mapsto|j_{2n}(\delta)_{i}|_{p}
is uniformly bounded in its valuation by some absolute constant
p^{C_{\operatorname{max}}}
, where . Since
\kappa|_{U}
is finite, the image of the closed subset
S\cap U=Z_{i_{1}}\cup\dots\cup Z_{i_{k}}
remains closed and nowhere Zariski-dense, so the set of “bad weights”
B=\kappa(S\cap U)\cup(V\cap Y)
is again proper closed in the irreducible
V
. Now we may consider the set
Q
of characters
\delta:T(\mathcal{O}_{F^{+},p})\to\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
regular dominant algebraic of weight
-w_{0}\lambda
such that for all
\tau:F^{+}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}

\displaystyle\lambda_{1,\tau}-\lambda_{2,\tau}>C_{\operatorname{max}}

\displaystyle\lambda_{i,\tau}-\lambda_{i+1,\tau}>\lambda_{i-1,\tau}-\lambda_{i,\tau}+(C_{\operatorname{max}}+1)

for some fixed absolute constant
C_{\operatorname{max}}
. By construction, every weight in
Q
is numerically noncritical. Moreover,
Q\cap(V\setminus B)
is Zariski dense in
V
, and thus by finiteness and equidimensionality
\kappa^{-1}(Q\cap(V\setminus B))\subset\mathcal{E}_{S,Y}
is Zariski-dense in
U
. This completes Step 1.
∎

5.14
Density of cuspidal classical points

We can now combine our work with
Corollary

2.31
and
Lemma

2.24
to prove the main theorem of this section.

Definition 5.15
.

We call a point
x=(y,\delta)\in\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}

sufficiently interior classical
if:

1.

y
lies in the support of the Hecke module
(J_{B}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p},L)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la}}))^{\prime}
;

2.

the character
\delta
is dominant algebraic for
\widetilde{G}
and CTG in the sense of
Definition

2.13
.

Corollary 5.16
.

\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
contains a Zariski-dense and accumulating set of points
(y,\delta)
of numerically non-critical sufficiently interior classical points.

Proof.

We apply
Proposition

5.13
in the case
S=\operatorname{supp}_{\mathcal{W}\times{\mathbf{G}}_{m}}\mathrm{H}^{d-1}(J_{B}(\Pi_{\partial,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{la}}))^{\prime}
, and
Y=\mathcal{W}_{\operatorname{non-CTG}}
. In particular, by
Corollary

2.31
we have that for any affinoid open subsets
U_{i}^{\prime}=\subset\mathcal{Z}_{c}^{d}:=\operatorname{supp}_{\mathcal{W}\times{\mathbf{G}}_{m}}\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d}
, we have
\pi_{\mathcal{W}}(S\cap U_{i}^{\prime}),\pi_{\mathcal{W}}(Y\cap U_{i}^{\prime})\subset\Omega:=\pi_{\mathcal{W}}(U_{i}^{\prime})
are proper Zariski–closed subsets in an affinoid, and are thus finite unions of irreducible components, each of which has dimension
<\dim\Omega=\dim\mathcal{W}
. Thus,
Proposition

5.13
applies and so the set
\mathcal{E}_{S,Y}
of points
x=(y,\delta)
with
\delta
locally dominant algebraic,
j_{2n}(\delta)
numerically noncritical whose weight also avoids the
S
and
Y
. By
Lemma

2.24
if
x=(y,\delta)\in\mathcal{E}_{S,Y}
, then
(\mathcal{M}_{\partial}^{d-1})_{x}=0
by Nakayama’s lemma (working locally around
f(y)
so that all the modules are finitely generated) and the definition of
S
, so
(\mathcal{M}_{c}^{d})_{x}\neq 0\implies(\mathcal{M}^{d})_{x}\neq 0
, and thus for any
x\in\mathcal{E}_{S,Y}
, we get a corresponding point
x\in\mathcal{E}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
on the middle degree eigenvariety. But then using numerical noncriticality, the classicality criterion
Lemma

5.9
implies
x
corresponds to a Hecke eigensystem in some
\mathrm{H}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p},\mathsf{L}(\lambda))_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
for
\lambda
a dominant weight for
\widetilde{G}
. By the CTG assumption on the weight
\lambda
(i.e.
Y
),
x
corresponds to a
cuspidal
automorphic representation for
\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+}})
.
∎

6
Trianguline Galois representations

We can now combine the density of classical (cuspidal) points in
\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
with degree shifting to derive our main theorem. First, recall that for
K,L/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
finite extensions a continuous local Galois representation
r:\operatorname{Gal}_{K}\to\operatorname{GL}_{n}(L)
is said to be
trianguline
of parameter
\delta:(K^{\times})^{n}\to L^{\times}
if the associated rigid
(\varphi,\Gamma_{K})
-module
D_{\operatorname{rig}}^{\dagger}(r)
has an increasing filtration by sub-
(\varphi,\Gamma_{K})
modules with rank
1
(ascending) graded pieces
\mathcal{R}_{L,K}(\delta_{1}),\dots,\mathcal{R}_{L,K}(\delta_{n})
.

Now we need to discuss Galois representations associated to classical automorphic forms for the unitary group
\widetilde{G}
, along with their local-global compatibility results. For shorthand, if
r:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to\operatorname{GL}_{n}(L)
is a continuous representation and
\nu\in S_{\ell}(F)
is a place, we let
r_{\nu}:=r|_{\operatorname{Gal}_{F_{\nu}}}
.

Theorem 6.1
.

Assume
F
contains an imaginary quadratic field
F_{0}
,
p
is a split prime in
F_{0}
, and
\pi
is a cuspidal automorphic representation of
\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F^{+}})
which
\xi
-cohomological for
\xi
an irreducible algebraic representation of
(\operatorname{Res}_{F^{+}/{\mathbf{Q}}}\widetilde{G})_{{\mathbf{C}}}
. Then for any isomorphism
\iota:\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}\simeq{\mathbf{C}}
there is an associated Galois representation
r_{\pi,\iota}:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to\operatorname{GL}_{2n}(\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})
satisfying:

1.

For any
\ell\neq p
which is unramified in
F
and above which
\pi
is unramified and for all
\nu\mid\ell
in
F
, we have
r_{\pi,\iota,\nu}
is unramified and the characteristic polynomial of
r_{\pi,\iota}(\operatorname{Frob}_{\nu})
is equal to the image of
\widetilde{P}_{\nu}(X)
in
\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}[X]
coming from the base change of
\iota^{-1}(\pi_{\overline{\nu}})
, where
\nu
lies above
\overline{\nu}\in S_{p}(F^{+})
.

2.

For any
\nu\mid p
in
F
we have
r_{\pi,\iota,\nu}
is de Rham with Hodge–Tate weights for each
\tau:F\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}
given by

\operatorname{HT}_{\tau}(r_{\pi,\iota,\nu})=\{\widetilde{\lambda}_{\tau,1}+2n-1,\widetilde{\lambda}_{\tau,2}+2n-2,\dots,\widetilde{\lambda}_{\tau,2n}\},

where
\widetilde{\lambda}\in({\mathbf{Z}}_{\operatorname{dom}}^{2n})^{\operatorname{Hom}(F,\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})}
is the highest weight of the representation
\iota^{-1}(\xi\otimes\xi)^{\vee}
of
\operatorname{GL}_{2n}/\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}
.

Suppose moreover that for each
\overline{\nu}|p
we have
\iota^{-1}\pi_{\overline{\nu}}\simeq\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{B}_{2n}(F_{\nu})}^{\operatorname{GL}_{2n}(F_{\nu})}\theta_{\overline{\nu}}
is an unramified principal series, and set
\theta:=\prod_{\overline{\nu}}\theta_{\overline{\nu}}
. Then we have:

(a)

r_{\pi,\iota,\nu}
is crystalline, and the Frobenius operator on
D_{\operatorname{crys}}(r_{\pi,\iota,\nu})
is semisimple with eigenvalues

\varphi_{\nu,i}=\theta_{\overline{\nu},i}(\varpi_{\nu})|\varpi_{\nu}|_{F_{\nu}}^{-i+1}.

(b)

Moreover if the refinement
j_{2n}(\theta\cdot\widetilde{\lambda}^{\vee})_{\overline{\nu}}
is numerically noncritical in the sense of
Definition

5.8
then
r_{\pi,\iota}|_{\operatorname{Gal}_{F_{\nu}}}
is trianguline with parameter
j_{2n}(\theta\cdot\widetilde{\lambda}^{\vee})_{\overline{\nu}}
.

Proof.

The theorem is essentially
[
ACC+23
, Thm. 2.3.3]
(which in turn is an adaptation of
[
Shi14
]
), once we note several consequences. Recall (a consequence of) the refined statement of local-global compatibility at finite places due to Caraiani (see
[
CN23
, Theorem 2.1.9 (3)]
): if
\ell
is prime which splits in
F_{0}
, then for each
\nu\in S_{p}(F)
in
F
lying above
\overline{\nu}\in S_{p}(F^{+})
, we have an isomorphism

\operatorname{WD}(r_{\pi,\iota}|_{\operatorname{Gal}_{F_{\nu}}})^{\operatorname{F}-ss}\simeq\iota^{-1}\operatorname{rec}_{F_{\nu}}(\pi_{\overline{\nu}}\otimes|\det|^{(1-2n)/2}).

Plugging in
\pi_{\overline{\nu}}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{B}_{2n}(F_{\nu})}^{\operatorname{GL}_{2n}(F_{\nu})}\theta_{\overline{\nu}}
to the right side yields

\displaystyle\operatorname{rec}_{F_{\nu}}(\pi_{\overline{\nu}}\otimes|\det|^{(1-2n)/2})

\displaystyle\simeq\theta_{\overline{\nu}}\delta_{B}^{1/2}|\cdot|_{F_{\nu}}^{(1-2n)/2}\circ\operatorname{Art}_{F_{\nu}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\theta_{\overline{\nu},1}|\cdot|_{F_{\nu}}^{(2n-1)/2},\theta_{\overline{\nu},2}|\cdot|_{F_{\nu}}^{(2n-3)/2},\dots,\theta_{\overline{\nu},2n}|\cdot|_{F_{\nu}}^{(1-2n)/2})|\cdot|_{F_{\nu}}^{(1-2n)/2}\circ\operatorname{Art}_{F_{\nu}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\theta_{\overline{\nu},1}\circ\operatorname{Art}_{F_{\nu}}^{-1},\theta_{\overline{\nu},2}|\cdot|_{F_{\nu}}^{-1}\circ\operatorname{Art}_{F_{\nu}}^{-1},\dots,\theta_{\overline{\nu},2n}|\cdot|_{F_{\nu}}^{1-2n}\circ\operatorname{Art}_{F_{\nu}}^{-1}),

so the eigenvalues for
\operatorname{Frob}_{\nu}
are
\theta_{\overline{\nu},1}(\varpi_{\nu}),\dots,\theta_{\overline{\nu},2n}(\varpi_{\nu})|\varpi_{\nu}|_{F_{\nu}}^{1-2n}
.

Then applying
[
NT21
, Lemma 2.8]
implies
r_{\pi,\iota}|_{\operatorname{Gal}_{F_{\nu}}}
is trianguline with parameter
\delta
defined by

\delta_{\nu,i}(x)=\theta_{\overline{\nu},i}(x)|x|_{F_{\nu}}^{1-i}\cdot\prod_{\tau}\tau(x)^{-\widetilde{\lambda}_{\tau,n-i+1}-i+1}

Now recalling the cyclotomic character
\varepsilon_{\operatorname{cyc}}\circ\operatorname{Art}_{F_{\nu}}=N_{F_{\nu}/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}(x)|x|_{F_{\nu}}
, we then have
N_{F_{\nu}/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}(x)^{1-i}=\prod_{\tau}\tau(x)^{1-i}=(\varepsilon_{\operatorname{cyc}}\circ\operatorname{Art}_{F_{\nu}}(x))^{1-i}|x|_{F_{\nu}}^{i-1}
. Thus
\delta_{\nu,i}=\theta_{\overline{\nu},i}\prod_{\tau}\tau(x)^{\widetilde{\lambda}_{\tau,n-i+1}}\cdot(\varepsilon_{\operatorname{cyc}}\circ\operatorname{Art}_{F_{\nu}})^{1-i}=j_{2n,i}(\theta\cdot\lambda^{\vee})_{\overline{\nu}}
. Thus,
r_{\pi,\iota}|_{\operatorname{Gal}_{F_{\nu}}}
is in fact trianguline of parameter
j_{2n}(\theta\cdot\lambda^{\vee})_{\overline{\nu}}
, as required.
∎

We recall certain deformation spaces of trianguline (pseudo)representations. Fix a continuous residual representation
\overline{r}:\operatorname{Gal}_{K}\to\operatorname{GL}_{n}(k_{L})
, let
R_{\overline{r}}^{\square}
be the associated framed Galois deformation ring, and let
X^{\square}(\overline{r})
be the rigid generic fibre of
\operatorname{Spf}(R_{\overline{r}}^{\square})
. Now let
\mathcal{T}_{L}^{n}:=\widehat{T_{n}(K)}_{L}
be the rigid space of continuous characters of
(K^{\times})^{n}
. Let
\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{reg}}^{n}\subset\mathcal{T}_{L}^{n}
be the Zariski dense open subset of characters
\delta=(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})
such that
\delta_{i}/\delta_{j}\neq z^{-\mathbf{k}},(\varepsilon_{\operatorname{cyc}}\circ\operatorname{Art}_{K})\cdot z^{\mathbf{k}}
for all
i\neq j
and
\mathbf{k}\in{\mathbf{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{\operatorname{Hom}(K,\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})}
. Now let
U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{r})=\{(r,\delta):X^{\square}(\overline{r})\times\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{reg}}^{n}:r\text{ is trianguline of parameter $\delta$}\}
. We then recall the
trianguline variety

X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{r})
is defined to be the Zariski closure of
U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{r})
in
X^{\square}(\overline{r})\times\mathcal{T}_{L}^{n}
. We could also make a similar definition taking representations at all places
\nu\mid p
at once. Basic structural results (see
[
BHS17
, Theoreme 2.6]
) tell us that
X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{r})
is quasi-Stein rigid space, equidimensional of dimension
n^{2}+\frac{n(n+1)}{2}[K:{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}]
and
U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{r})
is Zariski open, dense, and smooth, and the natural map
\omega:U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{r})\to\mathcal{W}_{L}
is also smooth.

Although
X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{r})
is defined by a closure, we still know the following about all the points in this space: if
x=(r,\delta)\in X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{r})
then by
[
KPX14
, Theorem 6.3.13]

r
is trianguline of some parameter
\delta^{\prime}=(\delta_{1}^{\prime},\dots,\delta_{n}^{\prime})
satisfying
\delta_{i}^{-1}\delta_{i}^{\prime}:K^{\times}\to\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
is an algebraic character. In particular, all representations on
X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{r})
are trianguline, but need not be trianguline of parameter the given character
\delta
. Note, however, that the weights of
\delta
and
\delta^{\prime}
must nevertheless coincide (see
[
BHS17
, Proposition 2.9]
).

Proposition 6.2
.

Keep the assumptions of
Theorem

6.1
.
If
z=(y,\delta=\delta_{\operatorname{sm}}\lambda^{\vee})\in\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
is a numerically noncritical sufficiently interior point of weight
\lambda
such that for all
\nu|p
of
F
the representation
\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{B}_{2n}(F_{\nu})}^{\operatorname{GL}_{2n}(F_{\nu})}(\delta_{\operatorname{sm},\overline{\nu}})
is absolutely irreducible, there is an associated Galois representation
r_{z}:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to\operatorname{GL}_{2n}(\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})
matching Frobenius and Hecke eigenvalues at unramified places, such that
r_{z}
is crystalline at
\nu|p
with Hodge–Tate weights given by
\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(j_{2n}(\delta_{z})_{\overline{\nu}})
and Frobenius eigenvalues given by
\delta_{\nu,i}(\varpi_{\nu})\prod_{\tau\in\operatorname{Hom}(F_{\nu},\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})}\tau(\varpi_{\nu})^{-\lambda_{\tau,2n-i+1}-i+1}
, and in particular
(r_{z},j_{2n}(\delta_{z})_{\overline{\nu}})\in U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}|_{\operatorname{Gal}_{F_{\nu}}})
.

Consequently, there is a pseudorepresentation
D:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d})
such that for any numerically noncritical sufficiently interior classical
z\in\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}(\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})
, the specialization
D_{z}:G_{F}\to\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d})\to\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}
is equal to
\operatorname{tr}r_{z}
.

Proof.

By definition and the discussion before
Definition

5.8
(taking into account that usual Jacquet module
J_{B}(\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{B}_{2n}(F_{\nu})}^{\operatorname{GL}_{2n}(F_{\nu})}\theta_{\overline{\nu}})
admits
\delta_{B}\cdot\theta_{\overline{\nu}}
as a subquotient),
z
takes the form
z=(\psi_{\pi},\delta_{\operatorname{sm}}\cdot\lambda^{\vee})
, where
\pi
is a cuspidal automorphic representation of
\widetilde{G}({\mathbb{A}}_{F}^{+})
of weight
\lambda
such that
\pi_{\overline{\nu}}\simeq\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{B}_{2n}(F_{\nu})}^{\operatorname{GL}_{2n}(F_{\nu})}\delta_{\operatorname{sm},\overline{\nu}}
. Thus, the first part of the proposition follows from
Theorem

6.1
.
Now let
{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d})^{\leq 1}\subset{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d})
be the subring of bounded elements.
The existence of the determinant
D
is a standard argument (see
[
Che04
, Proposition 7.1.1]
), which is a consequence of:
the Zariski density of noncritical sufficiently interior classical points, which implies the map
\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{E}_{c}^{d})^{\leq 1}\to\prod_{z\in Z}{\mathbf{C}}_{p}
is injective, the fact that
\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{E}_{c}^{d})^{\leq 1}
is compact, and the integrality of Hecke operators (i.e. the Hecke action
\psi:\widetilde{{\mathbf{T}}}^{S}\to\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{E}_{c}^{d})
factors through
\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{E}_{c}^{d})^{\leq 1}
). These facts imply that the trace of Frobenius at unramified primes of the product determinant
D_{\prod Z}:=\prod_{z}\det r_{z}
factors through
\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d})^{\leq 1}
.
[
Che14
, Example 2.32]
then implies we can glue determinants, and
D_{\prod Z}
factors through the desired determinant.
∎

The existence of the determinant
D:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d})
then yields a closed embedding
\iota_{c,\widetilde{G}}^{\prime}:\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}\hookrightarrow X_{\operatorname{ps}}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}})^{2n}\times\widehat{T(F_{p}^{+})}
. To keep in line with
Proposition

6.2
and match the weights, we define
\iota_{c,\widetilde{G}}:=(\operatorname{id}\times j_{2n})\circ\iota_{c,\widetilde{G}}^{\prime}
.
We would like to interpolate from classical points to say more about the image of
\iota_{c,\widetilde{G}}
. However, we run into the issue that
\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
only carries a
pseudorepresentation
, rather than an actual representation. Along these lines, we can similarly define the
trianguline pseudodeformation space

X_{\operatorname{ps},\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}},\nu})
to be the Zariski closure of the image of
\bigcup_{\overline{r}:\operatorname{tr}\overline{r}=\operatorname{tr}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}})_{\nu}}U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{r})
inside
X_{\operatorname{ps}}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}},\nu})\times\widehat{T(F_{p}^{+})}
. A version of this space was first studied by Hellmann
[
Hel12
]
(who called this a
finite slope space
), and in the case of
K={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
and
\overline{r}:\operatorname{Gal}_{K}\to\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}
a local residual pseudorepresentation he showed
X_{\operatorname{ps},\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{r})
is equidimensional of dimension
1+\frac{n(n+1)}{2}
. A refined consequence is the following:

Corollary 6.3
.

For all
\nu\in S_{p}(F)
, the composition
\operatorname{res}_{\nu}\circ\iota_{c,\widetilde{G}}:\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}\to X_{ps}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}})\times\widehat{T(F_{p}^{+})}\to X_{ps}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}},\nu})\times\widehat{T(F_{p}^{+})}
factors through
X_{\operatorname{ps},\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}},\nu})
.

Proof.

This is a consequence of
Proposition

6.2
, the Zariski-density of classical points, the definition of the trianguline pseudodeformation space, and the fact that
\iota_{c,\widetilde{G}}
is a closed embedding.
∎

For the sake of deducing results for
G=\operatorname{GL}_{n}/F
(for example, pointwise triangulinity), however, it is better to work with framed deformation spaces. So we need to pass from pseudorepresentations, to representations. We will do this by using
[
BC09
, Lemma 7.8.11]
by Bellaïche–Chenevier, and the following lemma.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
X
be a rigid space equipped with increasing admissible affinoid open cover
X=\bigcup_{n=1}^{\infty}U_{n}
and suppose
Z\subset X
is a Zariski-dense and accumulating subset. Let
V_{m}:=\overline{Z\cup U_{m}}\subset U_{m}
denote the Zariski-closure of the subset
Z\cap U_{m}
. Then for all
j\geq 1
, there exists
j^{\prime}\geq j
such that
U_{j}\subseteq V_{j^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

This claim follows from the proof of
[
CHJ17
, Lemma 5.9]
4
4

4

A separate proof was communicated by Brian Conrad. We thank him for also figuring out the shape of the lemma statement.
. The only difference there is that when
X
is normal (or smooth, as assumed in
loc. cit.
), the subsets
\overline{Z\cap U_{m}}
are a union of
connected
components of
V_{m}
, and are thus also affinoid open in
X
, implying the
V_{m}
themselves form an affinoid open cover.
∎

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
r:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to\operatorname{GL}_{m}(\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})
be a continuous representation. Then
r_{\nu}
is trianguline if and only if
(r^{\operatorname{ss}})_{\nu}
is trianguline. Moreover if
r_{\nu}
is trianguline of parameter
\delta
, then
(r^{\operatorname{ss}})_{\nu}
is also trianguline of parameter
\delta
.

Proof.

This result follows from the definition of triangulinity. Note of course if
(r^{\operatorname{ss}})_{\nu}
is trianguline of parameter
\delta
,
r_{\nu}
need not have parameter
\delta
.
∎

Proposition 6.6
.

If
x=(y,\delta)\in\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}(\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})
and
t=\iota_{c,\widetilde{G}}(x)=\operatorname{tr}\rho,
is the associated determinant for
\rho
semisimple, then
\rho|_{\operatorname{Gal}_{F_{\nu}}}
is a trianguline Galois representation with
\operatorname{HT}_{\tau}(\rho_{\nu})=\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(j_{2n}(\delta)_{\overline{\nu}})
and parameter
j_{2n}(\delta)_{\overline{\nu}}\cdot\delta^{\prime}
, for
\delta^{\prime}
an algebraic character.

Proof.

The idea is to reduce to the case of Galois representations. Since a densely pointwise strictly trianguline
(\varphi,\Gamma_{K})
-module over a rigid space
X
is globally pointwise trianguline by
[
KPX14
, 6.3.13]
, the corresponding statement is true upon replacing
\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}
with, say,
X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}},\nu})
. As such a statement is less clear for
X_{\operatorname{ps},\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}},\nu})
we instead work directly on the eigenvariety. Write
\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}=\cup_{m}U_{m}
as an increasing admissible cover of affinoid open subsets. Likewise let
Z\subset\mathcal{E}_{c,\mathfrak{m}}^{d}
be the set of numerically noncritical, sufficiently interior classical points, which is Zariski-dense and accumulating by
Corollary

5.16
.
If
x=(t,\delta)\in\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}(\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})\subset X_{ps,\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}})\times\widehat{T(F_{p}^{+})}
and
U_{m^{\prime}}\ni x
, then recall from
Lemma

6.4
that for some index
m\geq m^{\prime}
we have that
U_{m^{\prime}}\subset V_{m}:=\overline{U_{m}\cap Z}
, which is still an affinoid. The map
j_{m}:V_{m}\to X_{ps,\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}})
yields a rank
2n
pseudorepresentation
T:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to j_{m}^{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}}(V_{m})
. Then by
[
BC09
, Lemma 7.8.11]
there exists a reduced, separated, quasi-compact rigid space
Y
equipped with a proper, generically finite, surjective map
f:Y\to V_{m}
such that there exists a locally free coherent
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-module
\mathcal{M}
of rank
2n
with a continuous
\operatorname{Gal}_{F}
-action
R:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to\operatorname{GL}_{\mathcal{O}(Y)}(\mathcal{M})
, such that the trace
\operatorname{tr}R=f^{\ast}T
, and for any
y\in Y
the specialization
(M_{Y,y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y,y}}\overline{k(y)})^{ss}\simeq\rho_{f(y)}
, the semisimple representation corresponding to the point
f(y)\in V_{m}
. Moreover, in a Zariski-dense open subset of
Y
, the fibre
M_{Y,y}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{Y,y}}\overline{k(y)}
is automatically semisimple!

By the general theory in
[
KPX14
, Theorem 2.2.17]
we can attach to
M
a
(\varphi,\Gamma_{F_{\nu}})
-module
D_{\operatorname{rig}}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{M})
over the relative Robba ring
\mathcal{R}_{Y,F_{\nu}}
. Looking at the Zariski-closure
Y^{\prime}:=\overline{f^{-1}(Z\cap U_{m})}\subset Y
, we note the composite map
f:Y^{\prime}\to U_{m}
remains surjective (in particular,
x
is in the image!) since
f
is proper and thus maps closed analytic subsets to closed analytic subsets by
[
BGR84
, Chapter 9, Proposition 2]
. Thus renaming
Y^{\prime}
as
Y
, we may assume that the preimage of
Z\cap V_{m}
is Zariski-dense in
Y
.

Now the natural map
\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{d}\to\widehat{T(F_{p}^{+})}
, yields a morphism
Y\to V_{m}\xrightarrow{\pi_{T(F_{p}^{+})}\circ\iota_{c,\widetilde{G}}}\widehat{T(F_{p}^{+})}
, and thus produces universal characters

\chi=\begin{pmatrix}\chi_{1}&\\
&\ddots\\
&&\chi_{2n}\\
\end{pmatrix}:T(F_{p}^{+})\to\mathcal{O}(Y)^{\times}

By
Proposition

6.2
the specializations
(D_{\operatorname{rig}}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{M})_{Y,y}\otimes\overline{k(y)})^{\operatorname{ss}}
are strictly trianguline of parameter
\chi_{y}
for all
y\in f^{-1}(Z\cap U_{m})
, a Zariski dense subset. Moreover, restricting to the Zariski-dense
open
locus where the fibres
\mathcal{M}_{Y,y}\otimes\overline{k(y)}
are semisimple, we then get
D_{\operatorname{rig}}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{M})_{Y,y}
is densely pointwise strictly trianguline with parameter
\chi_{y}
. Thus we can apply
[
KPX14
, Theorem 6.3.13]
to get that
D_{\operatorname{rig}}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{M})
is pointwise trianguline at
all
points
x
, with parameter given by
\chi_{x}\cdot\delta_{x}
for
\delta_{x}
some algebraic character depending on
x
, such that
\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(\chi_{x}\cdot\delta_{x})=\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(\chi_{x})
for all
\tau:F_{\nu}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}
. Then
Lemma

6.5
implies for all
x\in V_{m}
that
\rho_{x,\nu}
is trianguline of parameter
\chi_{x}\cdot\delta_{x}
, as required.

∎

We can now return to our main interest,
G=\operatorname{Res}_{F/F^{+}}\operatorname{GL}_{n}
.
The pointwise triangulinity of eigenvarieties for
G
follows essentially immediately from
Proposition

6.6
and
Theorem

4.11
, but to derive statements on the triangulation or weights we need to introduce an extra degree of freedom in our framework.

Keep the assumptions of
Theorem

6.1
. Let
\overline{\nu}\in S_{p}(F^{+})
and
\nu,\nu^{c}\in S_{p}(F)
be the two places lying over
\overline{\nu}
(recall we are assuming
p
splits in the imaginary quadratic extension contained in
F
).
Then using
Theorem

4.11
we have all
i
and
w\in W^{\overline{P}}
such that
\ell(w)=d-1=i
, we have a diagram as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{c}^{d}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{ps}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}})\times\widehat{T(F_{p}^{+})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{ps}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}},\nu})\times\widehat{T(F_{\overline{\nu}}^{+})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{ps,\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}},\nu})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}^{i}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}})\times\widehat{T_{n}(F_{p})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu})\times\widehat{T_{n}(F_{\nu})})\times(X^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu^{c}})\times\widehat{T_{n}(F_{\nu^{c}})})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{c,\widetilde{G}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{w}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathfrak{m}}^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{res}_{\nu,\nu^{c}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{ps,w}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{ps,w,\overline{\nu}}}

(29)

In the diagram, the map
\operatorname{res}
is induced by the natural restriction maps
X^{\square,n}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}})\to X^{\square,n}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu})\times X^{\square,n}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu^{c}})
.
Let us spell out more explicitly the maps
d_{ps,w},d_{ps,w,\overline{\nu}}
.
Recall by construction, the embedding
j_{w}
sends a point
(x,\delta=(\delta_{\nu})_{\nu\in S_{p}(F)})\in(\operatorname{Spf}{\mathbf{T}}^{S}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}})^{\operatorname{rig}}\times\widehat{T_{n}(F_{p})}
to
(x\circ\mathcal{S},j_{w}(\delta))\in(\operatorname{Spf}\widetilde{{\mathbf{T}}}^{S}(\widetilde{K}^{p})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}})^{\operatorname{rig}}\times\widehat{T(F_{p}^{+})}
, where

(j_{w}(\delta))_{\overline{\nu}}=w^{-1}((\delta_{\nu},-w_{0}\delta_{\nu^{c}})+\rho_{\operatorname{cyc},\overline{\nu}}^{\widetilde{G}})-\rho_{\operatorname{cyc},\overline{\nu}}^{\widetilde{G}}

As we have shifted the two maps from the eigenvarieties to the deformation spaces via
\iota_{c,\widetilde{G}}(x,\delta)=(\operatorname{tr}(\rho_{x}),\delta+\rho_{\operatorname{cyc}}^{\widetilde{G}})
and
\iota_{\mathfrak{m}}^{i}(x,\delta)=(r_{x},\delta+\rho_{\operatorname{cyc}}^{G})
,
d_{ps,w}
is
defined
to be the map

\displaystyle(r,\delta)

\displaystyle\mapsto(\operatorname{tr}(r\oplus r^{c,\vee}(1-2n)),(w^{-1}((\delta_{\nu}-\rho_{\operatorname{cyc},\nu}^{G},-w_{0}(\delta_{\nu^{c}}-\rho_{\operatorname{cyc},\nu^{c}}^{G}))+\rho_{\operatorname{cyc}}^{\widetilde{G}}))_{\overline{\nu}})

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{tr}(r\oplus r^{c,\vee}(1-2n)),(w^{-1}(\delta_{\nu},-w_{0}\delta_{\nu^{c}}(1-2n)))_{\overline{\nu}},

where the second line follows from the fact that
\rho_{\operatorname{cyc},\overline{\nu}}^{\widetilde{G}}=(\rho_{\operatorname{cyc},\nu}^{G},\rho_{\operatorname{cyc},\nu}^{G}(-n))
.
We have some flexibility with the transfer map coming from twisting by characters, as in
[
ACC+23
, p.
37]
. Let
\chi:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{L}^{\times}
be a continuous character such that
\chi\circ\operatorname{Art}_{F_{\nu}}
is trivial on
\det(K_{\nu})
for each
\nu\notin S
of
F
. Then there is an isomorphism
f_{\chi}:\mathcal{H}(G^{S},K^{S})\to\mathcal{H}(G^{S},K^{S})
given by the formula
f_{\chi}(f)(g)=\chi(\operatorname{Art}_{F}(\det(g)))^{-1}f(g).
Then given
\mathfrak{m}\subset{\mathbf{T}}^{S}
a maximal ideal, we define
\mathfrak{m}(\chi)=f_{\chi}(\mathfrak{m})
. Moreover, let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi):=\mathcal{S}^{\ast}(\mathfrak{m}(\chi))
.
The next lemma tells us the effect of such a twisting on eigenvarieties. Let
\chi_{p}:\operatorname{GL}_{n}(F_{p})\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
be the character defined by
(g_{\nu})_{\nu\in S_{p}(F)}\mapsto\prod_{\nu\in S_{p}(F)}\chi(\operatorname{Art}_{F_{\nu}}(\det(g_{\nu})))
.

Lemma 6.7
.

There is a natural isomorphism of essentially admissible representations of
T(F_{p}^{+})

R_{\chi}:\mathrm{H}^{i}(J_{B}(\Pi(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\operatorname{la}}))\to\mathrm{H}^{i}(J_{B}(\Pi(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}(\chi)}^{\operatorname{la}}))\otimes_{L}\chi_{p},

which intertwines the actions of
\mathcal{H}(G^{S},K^{S})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Z}}}{\mathcal{O}}
on the left with the action of
\mathcal{H}(G^{S},K^{S})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Z}}}{\mathcal{O}}
by precomposing with
f_{\chi}
on the right.

As a result if
\mathfrak{m}\subset{\mathbf{T}}^{S}(K^{p})
is a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal and
\chi\mod\mathfrak{m}_{L}
is trivial then we have an isomorphism of rigid varieties
\mathcal{E}^{i}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}\simeq\mathcal{E}^{i}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}(\chi)}
fitting into a diagram
