\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

where the map
t_{\chi}:X^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}})\times\widehat{T_{n}(F_{p})}\to X^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}(\chi)})\times\widehat{T_{n}(F_{p})}
sends
(\rho,(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n}))\mapsto(\rho\otimes\chi,(\delta_{\nu,1}\otimes\chi\circ\operatorname{Art}_{F_{\nu}},\dots,\delta_{\nu,n}\otimes\chi\circ\operatorname{Art}_{F_{\nu}})_{\nu})

Proof.

By
[
Hev23
, Lemma 2.5]
, we have for any smooth left
K_{p}
-module
V
which is a finite free
{\mathcal{O}}/\pi^{m}
-module, there is an isomorphism

R\Gamma(X_{K},V)\to R\Gamma(X_{K},V,V\otimes\chi_{p})

which intertwines the natural action of
\mathcal{H}(G^{S},K^{S})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Z}}}{\mathcal{O}}
with the natural action precomposed with
f_{\chi}
. Moreover, the proof in
loc. cit.
implies if
V
has a bimodule structure, this isomorphism is equivariant for this structure. Passing to the dual by applying
\operatorname{RHom}(-,{\mathcal{O}}/\pi^{m})
yields a isomorphism
C_{\bullet}(X_{K^{p}})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Z}}[K_{p}]}V^{\vee}\to C_{\bullet}(X_{K^{p}})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Z}}[K_{p}]}V^{\vee}\otimes\chi_{p}^{-1}
in
\mathsf{D}({\mathcal{O}}/\pi^{m})
. Plugging in
V^{\vee}={\mathcal{O}}[K_{p}/K_{p}^{\prime}]
yields a
K_{p}
-equivariant isomorphism

C_{\bullet}(X_{K^{p}})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Z}}[K_{p}]}{\mathcal{O}}/\pi^{m}[K_{p}/K_{p}^{\prime}]\to C_{\bullet}(X_{K^{p}})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Z}}[K_{p}]}{\mathcal{O}}[K_{p}/K_{p}^{\prime}]\otimes\chi_{p}^{-1},

and passing to the derived limits over
K_{p}^{\prime}
and
m
yields an isomorphism

C_{\bullet}(X_{K^{p}})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Z}}[K_{p}]}{\mathcal{O}}[[K_{p}]]\to C_{\bullet}(X_{K^{p}})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Z}}[K_{p}]}{\mathcal{O}}[[K_{p}]]]\otimes\chi_{p}^{-1}
in
\mathsf{D}({\mathcal{O}}[[K_{p}]])

intertwining the actions of
\mathcal{H}(G^{S},K^{S})
via a twist by
f_{\chi}^{-1}
. Moreover, using the isomorphism
C_{\bullet}(X_{K^{p}})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Z}}[G]}{\mathcal{O}}[[G]]\to C_{\bullet}(X_{K^{p}})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Z}}[G]}{\mathcal{O}}[[G]]\otimes\chi_{p}^{-1}
, this map is in fact
G
-equivariant. Taking a Borel–Serre complex, we get a continuous
K_{p}
-linear homotopy equivalence

C_{\bullet}^{\operatorname{BS}}(X_{K^{p}})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Z}}[K_{p}]}D(K_{p})\to C_{\bullet}^{\operatorname{BS}}(X_{K^{p}})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Z}}[K_{p}]}D(K_{p})\otimes\chi_{p}^{-1}.

Passing to (Hausdorff)
N_{0}
-coinvariance, taking Hausdorff cohomology and finite slope parts yields the desired isomorphism

\mathrm{H}_{i}(J_{B}^{\vee}(C^{\operatorname{BS}}(K,D(K_{p}))))\simeq\mathrm{H}_{i}(J_{B}^{\vee}(C^{\operatorname{BS}}(K,D(K_{p})\otimes\chi_{p}^{-1})))
with the desired intertwining of Hecke actions. Running the same argument for the complexes localized at
\mathfrak{m}
yields an isomorphism

\mathrm{H}_{i}(J_{B}^{\vee}(C^{\operatorname{BS}}(K,D(K_{p}))))_{\mathfrak{m}}\simeq\mathrm{H}_{i}(J_{B}^{\vee}(C^{\operatorname{BS}}(K,D(K_{p})\otimes\chi_{p}^{-1})))_{\mathfrak{m}}
, where the Hecke action on the right is twisted by
f_{\chi}^{-1}.
Passing to the associated Hecke algebras an eigenvarieties, we get that the map on Hecke algebras
f_{\chi}:\mathcal{H}^{S}(G^{S},K^{S})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Z}}}{\mathcal{O}}\to\mathcal{H}^{S}(G^{S},K^{S})\otimes_{{\mathbf{Z}}}{\mathcal{O}}
induces an isomorphism of rigid spaces
f_{\chi}^{\ast}:\operatorname{Spf}T^{S}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}(\chi)}\times\widehat{T_{n}(F_{p})}\to\operatorname{Spf}T^{S}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}\times\widehat{T_{n}(F_{p})}
which sends
(x:{\mathbf{T}}^{S}(K^{p})\to\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p},\delta=(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n}))\mapsto(x\circ f_{\chi},(\delta_{1,\nu}\otimes\chi_{\nu}\circ\operatorname{Art}_{F_{\nu}},\dots,\delta_{n,\nu}\otimes\chi_{\nu}\circ\operatorname{Art}_{F_{\nu}})_{\nu})
. The isomorphism we have just construct then implies that this map descends to an isomorphism

f_{\chi}^{\ast}:E^{i}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}(\chi)}\simeq E^{i}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}.

We then define
\operatorname{tw}_{\chi}=f_{\chi}^{-1}
. The resulting effect on Galois representations (i.e. the diagram involving the Galois deformations) follows from the construction.
∎

Note that for any
\chi
as above, we get an induced (framed) twisted variant of
d_{ps,w}
which we denote
d_{w,\chi}^{\prime}:X^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}(\chi)})\times\widehat{T}\hookrightarrow X^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi)})\times\widehat{T}
. On representations, this map concretely sends
\rho\otimes\chi\mapsto\rho\otimes\chi\oplus\rho^{c,\vee}\otimes\chi^{c,\vee}(1-2n)
. Note that this map only depends on the reduction
\overline{\chi}
. Twisting this map by
\chi^{-1}
, we then have a map
d_{\chi}:X^{\square,n}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}})\hookrightarrow X^{\square,2n}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi)}(\chi^{-1}))
. Since any character twist of a (strictly) trianguline representation remains (strictly) trianguline, for any
\chi
as above we have
\operatorname{tr}\circ\operatorname{res}_{\nu}\circ d_{\chi}\circ\iota_{\mathfrak{m}(\chi)}^{i}:\mathcal{E}^{i}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}\to X_{ps}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi)}(\chi^{-1})|_{\operatorname{Gal}_{F_{\nu}}})\times\widehat{T}
naturally factors through the trianguline pseudodeformation space

X_{ps,\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi)}(\chi^{-1}))
.
Now recall the shape of algebraic Hecke characters of a CM field. Namely, if
\chi:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
is (the Galois representation corresponding to) an algebraic Hecke character of weight
\lambda\in{\mathbf{Z}}^{\operatorname{Hom}(F,\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})}
, then there exists
w\in{\mathbf{Z}}
such that
\lambda_{\tau}+\lambda_{\tau c}=w
for all
\tau
. In particular,
\chi\cdot\chi^{c}
has constant weight
w
. We now derive the main theorem of the paper:

Proof of
Theorem

1.1
.

The pointwise triangulinity follows by combining
Proposition

6.6
and
Theorem

4.11
. To pin down the triangulation and weights, the idea is to twist the eigenvariety
\mathcal{E}^{i}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}
by genuinely
p
-adic, non-integral weights using
Lemma

6.7
. For a given point
(x,\delta)\in\mathcal{E}^{i}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}
we claim we can choose distinct continuous characters
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{L}^{\times}
(taking
L/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
sufficiently large) such that
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}
are trivial
\mod\mathfrak{m}_{L}
and the following hold:

•

for each
\nu\in S_{p}(F)
and
\tau:F_{\nu}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}
, the representations
\rho_{x},\chi_{1}^{-1}\chi_{1}^{c,\vee}\rho_{x}^{c,\vee}(1-2n),\chi_{2}^{-1}\chi_{2}^{c,\vee}\rho_{x}^{c,\vee}(1-2n)
pairwise have no
\tau
-Hodge-Tate-Sen weights differing by an integer.

•

for each
\nu\in S_{p}(F)
the characters
\delta_{v},-w_{0}^{G}\chi_{1,\nu}^{-1}\chi_{1,\nu^{c}}^{\vee}\delta_{v^{c}}(1-2n),-w_{0}^{G}\chi_{2,\nu}^{-1}\chi_{2,\nu^{c}}^{\vee}\delta_{v^{c}}(1-2n)\in\widehat{T_{n}(F_{\nu})}
are such that no two pairwise characters differ by an algebraic character.

Indeed, it suffices to show that we can choose
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}
so that for any
\tau:F_{\nu}\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}
the characters
a_{1}:=\chi_{1,\nu}^{-1}\chi_{1,\nu^{c}}^{\vee},a_{2}:=\chi_{2,\nu}^{-1}\chi_{2,\nu^{c}}^{\vee}
have
\tau
-Sen weights such that
\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(a_{1}),\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(a_{2}),\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(a_{1}a_{2}^{-1})
are all non-integral and
\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(a_{1}),\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(a_{2})
avoid the discrete countable set
\{({\mathbf{Z}}+\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(\rho_{x,\nu})_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(\rho_{x,\nu^{c}}^{\vee})_{j})_{1\leq i,j\leq n},({\mathbf{Z}}+\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(j_{n}(\delta)_{\nu,i})-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(j_{n}(\delta)_{v^{c},j}))_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\}\subset{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}
. But by the above description of algebraic Hecke characters (in particular, composing the norm character with
p
-adic weights of
{\mathcal{O}}_{L}^{\times}
), after passing to all possible
p
-adic characters we have for any
\lambda
in an open subgroup of
{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}
there exists a continuous character
\chi
with
\chi^{-1}\chi^{c,\vee}
having constant weight
\lambda
. As such, we simply choose
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}
so that
a_{1},a_{2}
have nonintegral weights whose difference is also nonintegral, and
a_{1},a_{2}
avoid the sets mentioned above, which is possible since
{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}
is uncountable.

Let
\chi
be either
\chi_{1}
or
\chi_{2}
as just constructed. Now, the proof of
Proposition

6.6
implies for any
z=(x_{z},\delta_{z})\in\mathcal{E}_{c,\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi)}^{d}
there is a (not necessarily open) affinoid
U_{z}
, a proper surjective map
f_{z}:Y_{z}\to U_{z}
, and a Galois representation
R_{z}
on a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{Y_{z}}
-module
M
of rank
2n
such that
\operatorname{tr}R_{z}=f_{z}^{\ast}T|_{U_{z}}
, and for all
y\in Y_{z}
, the specialization
(M_{Y_{z},y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y_{z},y}}\overline{k(y)})^{\operatorname{ss}}\simeq\rho_{f_{z}(y)}
, and in fact for all
y
, the specialization
(M_{Y_{x},y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y_{x},y}}\overline{k(y)})^{\operatorname{ss}}\simeq\rho_{f_{z}(y)}
is trianguline at
\nu\mid p
of parameter
j_{2n}(\delta_{f_{z}(y)})_{\overline{\nu}}\delta^{\prime}
for
\delta^{\prime}
some algebraic character depending on
y
. Thus via diagram (
29
) we then have for any
\chi:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
satisfying the conditions in
Lemma

6.7
we have for any
(x,\delta)\in\mathcal{E}^{i}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}
that
\rho_{x}\oplus\chi^{-1}\chi^{c,\vee}\rho_{x}^{c,\vee}(1-2n)
is trianguline of parameter
(\delta_{\overline{\nu}}^{\prime}\cdot w^{-1}(j_{n}(\delta)_{\nu},-w_{0}(j_{n}(\delta)_{\nu^{c}})(1-2n)\cdot(\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}})^{-1}\circ\operatorname{Art}_{F_{\nu}}))_{\overline{\nu}}\in\widehat{T(F_{p}^{+})}
, for
\delta^{\prime}
an algebraic character.

Note since
w
is by construction a minimal length representative, if
j_{n}(\delta)_{\nu}=(\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n})
and
-w_{0}j_{n}(\delta_{\nu^{c}})=(t_{1},\dots,t_{n})
, then the relative order of the
\delta_{i}
(respectively, the order amongst the
t_{i}
) is preserved within the conjugated character
d_{ps,w,\chi}(j_{n}(\delta)):=w^{-1}(j_{n}(\delta)_{\nu},-w_{0}(j_{n}(\delta)_{\nu^{c}})(1-2n)\cdot(\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}})^{-1}\circ\operatorname{Art}_{F_{\nu}}))_{\overline{\nu}}
. Thus, if
\mathcal{F}_{\bullet,\chi_{1}}
(respectively,
\mathcal{F}_{\bullet,\chi_{2}}
) is a triangulation of
D_{\operatorname{rig}}^{\dagger}(\rho_{x})\oplus D_{\operatorname{rig}}^{\dagger}(\chi_{1,\nu}^{-1}\chi_{1,\nu^{c}}^{-1}\otimes\rho_{x,v^{c}}^{\vee}(1-2n))
(respectively,
D_{\operatorname{rig}}^{\dagger}(\rho_{x})\oplus D_{\operatorname{rig}}^{\dagger}(\chi_{2,\nu}^{-1}\chi_{2,\nu^{c}}^{-1}\otimes\rho_{x,v^{c}}^{\vee}(1-2n))
with graded pieces given up to twists by algebraic characters by
d_{ps,w,\chi_{1}}(j_{n}(\delta))
(resp,
d_{ps,w,\chi_{1}}(j_{n}(\delta))
, then by considering the image of the filtrations when projecting down to
D_{\operatorname{rig}}^{\dagger}(\rho_{x})
for both
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}
, the resulting filtration must have graded pieces of which, up to multiplying by algebraic characters, are in the intersection
\{d_{ps,w,\chi_{1}}(j_{n}(\delta))_{i}\}\cap\{d_{ps,w,\chi_{1}}(j_{n}(\delta))_{i}\}=\{j_{n}(\delta_{i})\}
by the assumptions on the characters
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}
and
j_{n}(\delta)
are in the correct order since
w\in W^{\overline{P}}
. The statement about the Hodge–Tate–Sen weights follows similarly, since by
Proposition

6.6
we know the
\tau
-Sen weights of
\rho_{x}\oplus\rho_{x}^{c,\vee}(1-2n)\chi_{i}^{-1}\chi_{i}^{c,\vee}
for the characters
\chi_{i}:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{L}^{\times}
.
∎

6.8
Towards a deformation-theoretic statement

We would like to have a more deformation-theoretic version of
Theorem

1.1
, so that we can study eigenvarieties via spaces of trianguline representations as in
[
BHS19
]
. In essence, our proof of
Theorem

1.1
came from studying maps

\displaystyle d_{w,\chi,\overline{\nu}}:X^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu})\times\widehat{T_{n}(F_{\nu})}\times X^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu^{c}})\times\widehat{T_{n}(F_{\nu^{c}})}\to X^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\overline{\chi}),\nu}(\overline{\chi}_{\nu}^{-1}))\times\widehat{T(F_{\overline{\nu}}^{+})}

\displaystyle((r_{\nu},\delta_{\nu}),(r_{\nu^{c}},\delta_{\nu^{c}}))\mapsto(r_{\nu}\oplus r_{\nu^{c}}^{\vee}(1-2n)(\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu}^{c})^{-1},w^{-1}(\delta_{\nu},-w_{0}^{G}\delta_{\nu^{c}}(1-2n)(\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu}^{c})^{-1})).

In actuality, we considered this map after taking the pseudocharacter, we which we denoted
d_{ps,w,\chi,\overline{\nu}}
. Note of course then
d_{ps,w,\chi,\overline{\nu}}=\operatorname{tr}\circ d_{w,\chi,\overline{\nu}}
.
a natural strategy is to constrain the preimage
P_{ps,\chi}:=d_{ps,w,\chi,\overline{\nu}}^{-1}(X_{ps,\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\overline{\chi}),\nu}(\overline{\chi}_{\nu}^{-1})))\subset X^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu})\times\widehat{T_{n}(F_{\nu})}\times X^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu^{c}})\times\widehat{T_{n}(F_{\nu^{c}})}
, since in (
29
) the map
\operatorname{res}_{\nu,\nu^{c}}\circ\iota_{\mathfrak{m}}^{i}
from
\mathcal{E}^{i}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}
factors through
P_{ps,\chi}
.

Lemma 6.9
.

We have an inclusion
P_{ps,\chi}\supset X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m,\nu}})\times X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m,\nu^{c}}})
.

Proof.

Letting
c_{w,\chi}:\widehat{T(F_{\overline{\nu}}^{+})}\simeq\widehat{T(F_{\overline{\nu}}^{+})}
denote the isomorphism sending
(\delta_{\nu},-w_{0}\delta_{\nu^{c}})\to w^{-1}(\delta,-w_{0}\delta_{\nu^{c}}(1-2n)(\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}})^{-1})
.
Note that we have Zariski dense and open inclusions
c_{w,\chi}^{-1}(\widehat{T(F_{\overline{\nu}}^{+})}_{\operatorname{reg}})\subset\widehat{T_{n}(F_{\nu})}_{\operatorname{reg}}\times\widehat{T_{n}(F_{\nu^{c}})}_{\operatorname{reg}}\subset\widehat{T(F_{\overline{\nu}}^{+})}
.

Let
U_{w,\chi}:=\{((r_{1},\delta_{1}),(r_{2},\delta_{2}))\in U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m,\nu}})\times U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m,\nu^{c}}}):c_{w,\chi}(\delta_{1},-w_{0}^{G}\delta_{2})\in\widehat{T(F_{\overline{\nu}}^{+})}_{\operatorname{reg}}\}
be the pullback of
U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m,\nu}})\times U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m,\nu^{c}}})
to
c_{w,\chi}^{-1}(\widehat{T(F_{\overline{\nu}}^{+})}_{\operatorname{reg}})
. Then since the morphism
U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m,\nu}})\times U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m,\nu^{c}}})\to\widehat{T(F_{\overline{\nu}}^{+})}
is smooth and
c_{w,\chi}^{-1}(\widehat{T(F_{\overline{\nu}}^{+})}_{\operatorname{reg}})\subset\widehat{T_{n}(F_{\nu})}_{\operatorname{reg}}\times\widehat{T_{n}(F_{\nu^{c}})}_{\operatorname{reg}}
is Zariski dense and open, the
U_{w,\chi}
is also Zariski dense and open in
U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m,\nu}})\times U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m,\nu^{c}}})
. Since
P_{ps,\chi}
is by construction Zariski-closed in (the product of) deformation spaces, it contains the Zariski-closure of
U_{w}
, which is
X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m,\nu}})\times X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m,\nu^{c}}})
.
∎

Note that
\operatorname{tr}(X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\overline{\chi}),\nu}(\overline{\chi}_{\nu}^{-1})))\subset X_{ps,\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\overline{\chi}),\nu}(\overline{\chi}_{\nu}^{-1}))
, and thus we have an inclusion of Zariski closed subspaces
d_{w,\chi,\overline{\nu}}^{-1}(X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\overline{\chi}),\nu}(\overline{\chi}_{\nu}^{-1})))=:P_{\chi}\subset P_{ps,\chi}
for any
\chi
. Moreover, the same proof as in
Lemma

6.9
shows
P_{\chi}\supset X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu})\times X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu^{c}})
. Ultimately, we would like to reduce the study of
P_{ps,\chi}
to
P_{\chi}
, since
X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}
has been more thoroughly studied. We end the article by studying
P_{\chi}
and discuss some lemmas that might be helpful in showing
P_{\chi}\subset P_{ps,\chi}
might actually be an equality.

Lemma 6.10
.

Let
\rho:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to\operatorname{GL}_{2n}({\mathcal{O}}_{L})
be a continuous representation such that
\overline{\rho}^{\operatorname{ss}}\simeq\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}\oplus\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{c,\vee}(1-2n)
, where
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to\operatorname{GL}_{n}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
is an absolutely irreducible representation. Then if
(\rho|_{\operatorname{Gal}_{F_{\nu}}},\delta)\in X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}},\nu})
, we also have
(\rho^{\operatorname{ss}}|_{\operatorname{Gal}_{F_{\nu}}},\delta)\in X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}},\nu})
.

Proof.

If
\rho
is irreducible, then it is automatically semisimple, and there is nothing to prove. Otherwise, we have
\rho^{ss}\simeq V\oplus W
, where
V
and
W
are lifts of the irreducible representations
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}},\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{c,\vee}
, respectively. Suppose WLOG that
V
is a subrepresentation of
\rho
. As a result,
\rho
is conjugate to a representation of the form

\begin{pmatrix}V&\ast\\
0&W\end{pmatrix},

for some potentially nontrivial
\ast
. Now for all
t
, we may consider the auxiliary representation

\rho_{t}:=\begin{pmatrix}V&t\cdot\ast\\
0&W\end{pmatrix}:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[[t]])

For
t\neq 0
, we have
\rho_{t}\simeq\rho
, as they are conjugate by the matrix
\begin{pmatrix}t\cdot I_{n}&0\\
0&I_{n}\end{pmatrix}.

The universal property of the deformation space then gives a map

f:(\operatorname{Spf}({\mathcal{O}}_{L}[[t]]))^{\operatorname{rig}}\to X^{\square,2n}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}},\nu})\times\widehat{T(F_{p}^{+})},

where on the second factor it is just the constant map
\delta\in\widehat{T}
. We know that restricting to
f|_{(\operatorname{Spf}({\mathbf{Z}}_{p}[[t]]))^{\operatorname{rig}}\setminus\{0\}}
, the image
(\rho_{t},\delta)\in X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu})
since
(\rho,\delta)\in X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square,2n}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}})
and conjugation by
\begin{pmatrix}t\cdot I_{n}&0\\
0&I_{n}\end{pmatrix}
is an automorphism on the trianguline variety. As
X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}})
is Zariski-closed and
(\operatorname{Spf}({\mathcal{O}}_{L}[[t]]))^{\operatorname{rig}}\setminus\{0\}
is a dense open in
\operatorname{Spf}({\mathcal{O}}_{L}[[t]])
, we have a factorization
f:\operatorname{Spf}({\mathcal{O}}_{L}[[t]])\to X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}})
. Specializing, to
t=0
, we have
\rho_{0}\simeq\begin{pmatrix}V&0\\
0&W\end{pmatrix}\simeq\rho^{ss}
and thus
(\rho^{\operatorname{ss}},\delta)\in X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}},\nu})
, as required.
∎

We now study
P_{\chi}
for varying
\chi
. In particular, for all characters
\chi:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{L}^{\times}
as in
Lemma

6.7
we are led to study
W:=\bigcap_{\chi}P_{\chi}\supset X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu})\times X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu^{c}})
. To get a grasp on this intersection, we first look at the open dense subset
U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi),\nu}(\overline{\chi}_{\nu}^{-1}))
for varying
\chi
.

Lemma 6.11
.

There is an inclusion
\bigcap_{\chi}d_{w,\chi,\overline{\nu}}^{-1}(U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi),\nu}(\overline{\chi}_{\nu}^{-1})))\subset U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu})\times U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu^{c}})
. In fact for any
x=((\rho_{x,\nu},\delta_{\nu}),(\rho_{x,\nu},\delta_{\nu^{c}}))
, there exists two continuous characters
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{L}^{\times}
such that if
d_{w,\chi_{1},\overline{\nu}}(x)\in U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi_{1}),\nu}(\overline{\chi}_{1,\nu}^{-1}))
and
d_{w,\chi_{2},\overline{\nu}}(x)\in U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi_{2}),\nu}(\overline{\chi}_{2,\nu}^{-1}))
, then
x\in U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu})\times U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu^{c}})
.

Proof.

We are tasked with showing that if
\rho_{x,\nu}\oplus\chi_{\nu}^{-1}\chi_{\nu^{c}}^{-1}\rho_{x,\nu^{c}}^{\vee}(1-2n)
is trianguline of parameter
w^{-1}(\delta_{\nu},(-w_{0}\delta_{\nu^{c}})(1-2n)\chi_{\nu}^{-1}\chi_{\nu^{c}}^{-1})\in\widehat{T(F_{\overline{\nu}}^{+})}
for many
\chi
then
\rho_{x,\nu}
is trianguline of parameter
\delta\in\widehat{T_{n}(F_{\nu})}_{\operatorname{reg}}
.
We follow the same strategy as in the proof of
Theorem

1.1
, where we choose two characters
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}
such that
\rho,\rho^{c,\vee}\otimes\chi_{1}^{c,\vee}\chi_{1}^{-1}(1-2n),\rho^{c,\vee}\otimes\chi_{2}^{c,\vee}\chi_{2}^{-1}(1-2n)
have no pairwise Sen weights (between each of the representations) differing by an integer, and same for the triangulations. If both
w^{-1}(\delta_{\nu},(-w_{0}\delta_{\nu^{c}})(1-2n)\chi_{1,\nu}^{-1}\chi_{1,\nu^{c}}^{-1})
and
w^{-1}(\delta_{\nu},(-w_{0}\delta_{\nu^{c}})(1-2n)\chi_{2,\nu}^{-1}\chi_{2,\nu^{c}}^{-1})
are regular parameters, then these triangulations are unique. Then by considering the filtrations
\mathcal{F}_{\bullet,\chi_{i}}
on
D_{\operatorname{rig}}^{\dagger}(\rho_{x,\nu})\oplus D_{\operatorname{rig}}^{\dagger}(\rho_{x,\nu^{c}}^{\vee}\otimes\chi_{i,\nu^{c}}^{-1}\chi_{i,\nu}^{-1}(1-2n))
, the weights of the parameters/subquotients of the triangulation of which map nontrivially to (a subquotient of)
D_{\operatorname{rig}}^{\dagger}(\rho)
must be contained in the intersection of the sets
\{(\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(\delta_{\nu,i}))_{i},(\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(\delta_{\nu^{c},j}\chi_{1}^{c,\vee}\chi_{1}^{-1}(1-2n)))_{j}\}\cap\{(\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(\delta_{\nu,i}))_{i},-(\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}((\delta_{\nu^{c},j}\chi_{2}^{c,\vee}\chi_{2}^{-1}(1-2n)))_{j}\}=\{(\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(\delta_{i}))_{i}\}
, so by fact that the pairwise weights cannot differ by integers, only the
\mathcal{R}_{L}(\delta_{\nu,i})
can admit non-zero maps to
\rho_{x,\nu}
, and they give a triangulation. We then similarly get a triangulation on
\rho_{x,\nu^{c}}^{\vee}
, and thus on
\rho_{x,\nu^{c}}
.
∎

We end with discussing two other examples.

Example 6.12

(
n=2
,
F
imaginary quadratic)
.

We can try to pin down precisely the conditions we need in the Bianchi case. Here,
F^{+}={\mathbf{Q}}
and
p=\nu\nu^{c}
is split in
F
. Thus in this case,
T(F_{\overline{\nu}}^{+})=T_{2}(F_{\nu})\times T_{2}(F_{\nu^{c}})\simeq({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times})^{4}
. We can also say a little more about the element
w\in W^{\overline{P}}
. Since we are localizing at a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal and
F
is imaginary quadratic, the only degrees of cohomology we are interested in are
i=1,2
. In this case the middle degree
d=4
, so the lengths of the needed Weyl group representatives are
\ell(w_{1})=2,\ell(w_{1})=1
. In the set
W^{\overline{P}}\simeq(S_{2}\times S_{2})\backslash S_{4}
, two such representatives are
w_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}&1&&\\
&&1&\\
1&&&\\
&&&1\end{pmatrix}
and
w_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}1&&&\\
&&1&\\
&1&&\\
&&&1\end{pmatrix}
.
Given
(e_{1},e_{2},f_{1},f_{2})\in\widehat{T(F_{\overline{\nu}}^{+})}
, we then have
w_{1}^{-1}(e_{1},e_{2},f_{1},f_{2})=(f_{1},e_{1},e_{2},f_{2})
and
w_{2}^{-1}(e_{1},e_{2},f_{1},f_{2})=(e_{1},f_{1},e_{2},f_{2})
. For simplicity let us focus on the case of
w=w_{1}
, which would correspond to studying
\mathrm{H}^{1}(J_{B_{2}}(\Pi(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\operatorname{la}}))\simeq J_{B_{2}}(\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K^{p},L)_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\operatorname{la}})
, this equality holding since one can show by hand that
\widetilde{\mathrm{H}}^{0}(K^{p},L)_{\mathfrak{m}}
is Eisenstein.
In this case we study a variant of the map
d_{w_{1},\chi,\overline{\nu}}
from before, defined as

d_{\chi}:X^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu})\times\widehat{({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times})^{2}}\times X^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu^{c}})\times\widehat{({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times})^{2}}\hookrightarrow X^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi),\nu}(3)\overline{\chi}_{\nu^{c}})\times\widehat{({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times})^{4}}

\displaystyle(\rho_{\nu},(\delta_{\nu,1},\delta_{\nu,2}),\rho_{\nu^{c}},(\delta_{\nu^{c},1},\delta_{\nu^{c},2}))

\displaystyle\mapsto(\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}}\rho_{\nu}(3)\oplus\rho_{\nu^{c}}^{\vee},d_{\chi}(\delta))

\displaystyle d_{\chi}(\delta)

\displaystyle:=(-\delta_{\nu^{c},2},\delta_{\nu,1}\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}}(3),\delta_{\nu,2}\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}}(3),-\delta_{\nu^{c},1})

We are interested in the preimage of
X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi),\nu}(3)\overline{\chi}_{\nu^{c}})
under
d_{\chi}
for various characters
\chi
. By
[
KPX14
, Theorem 6.3.13]
, if
(\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}}\rho_{\nu}(3)\oplus\rho_{\nu^{c}}^{\vee},d_{\chi}(\delta))\in X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi),\nu}(3)\overline{\chi}_{\nu^{c}})
then
\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}}\rho_{\nu}(3)\oplus\rho_{\nu^{c}}^{\vee}
is trianguline of parameter
d_{\chi}(\delta)\cdot(z^{a},z^{b},z^{c},z^{d})
where
a,b,c,d\in{\mathbf{Z}}
satisfy
a+b+c+d=0
. Choosing
\chi_{1},\chi_{2}
appropriately as in the proof of
Theorem

1.1
, we may assume that
\rho_{\nu^{c}}^{\vee}
is trianguline of parameters
-w_{0}\delta_{\nu^{c}}=(-\delta_{\nu^{c},2},-\delta_{\nu^{c},2})\cdot(z^{r},z^{s})
for some
r+s=0
, and
\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu}^{-1}\delta_{\nu,i}\delta_{\nu^{c},j}
has non-integral weight for
1\leq i,j\leq 2
. In particular, the non-zero map
\mathcal{R}_{L,{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}(-\delta_{\nu^{c},2}z^{a})\hookrightarrow D_{\operatorname{rig}}^{\dagger}(\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}}\rho_{\nu}(3)\oplus\rho_{\nu^{c}}^{\vee})
induces a nonzero map
\mathcal{R}_{L,{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}(-\delta_{\nu^{c},2}z^{a})\to D_{\operatorname{rig}}^{\dagger}(\rho_{\nu^{c}}^{\vee})
. Similarly, there is a nonzero map
D_{\operatorname{rig}}^{\dagger}(\rho_{\nu^{c}}^{\vee})\to\mathcal{R}_{L,{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}(-\delta_{\nu^{c},1}z^{d})
through which the triangulation on
\rho_{\nu}\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}}(2n-1)\oplus\rho_{\nu^{c}}
factors. This gives a resulting triangulation of
\rho_{\nu^{c}}^{\vee}
, and thus
a+d=0
. Moreover, by
[
CNT24
, Lemma 3.9]
we in fact have
a\leq 0
. Then by
[
KPX14
, Proposition 6.2.8]
there is a nonzero map
\mathcal{R}_{L,{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}(-\delta_{\nu^{c},2})\to\mathcal{R}_{L,{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}(-\delta_{\nu^{c},2}\cdot z^{a})
, and thus a nonzero map
\mathcal{R}_{L,{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}(-\delta_{\nu^{c},2})\to D_{\operatorname{rig}}^{\dagger}(\rho_{\nu^{c}}^{\vee})
. Then by dualizing, to show
(\rho_{\nu^{c}},\delta_{\nu^{c}})\in X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu^{c}})
, we are reduced to the following question.

Conjecture 1
.

When
F_{\nu}={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
, we have
(\rho,\delta_{1},\delta_{2})\in X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu^{c}})
if and only if there is a nonzero map
D_{\operatorname{rig}}^{\dagger}(\rho)\to\mathcal{R}_{L,{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}(\delta_{2})
,
\det\rho=\delta_{1}\delta_{2}
, and with
\tau
-Sen weights
(\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(\delta_{1}),\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(\delta_{2}))
.

This conjecture would imply at least that the projection of
\bigcap_{\chi}d_{\chi}^{-1}(X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi),\nu}(3)\overline{\chi}_{\nu^{c}}))
to deformation space at
\nu^{c}
lands in
X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu^{c}})
. By symmetry, a similar result would also hold for
\nu
.

6.13
The case of generic crystalline representations

A key case in which there is strong control on points of the trianguline variety comes from the local models of
[
BHS19
]
, and the extension to non-regular representations by
[
Wu24
]
. Let
r:\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{K}/K)\to\operatorname{GL}_{n}(\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})
be a crystalline representation, and denote its reduction by
\overline{r}
. Associated to
r
then is the rank
n

\varphi
-module
D_{\operatorname{crys}}(r)
over
L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}K_{0}
, where
K_{0}\subset K
denotes the maximal unramified subextension. Fixing an embedding
\tau:K_{0}\hookrightarrow L
, let
\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{n}
be eigenvalues of
\varphi^{[K_{0}:{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}]}
on
L\otimes_{1\otimes\tau_{0},L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}K_{0}}D_{\operatorname{crys}}(r)
. We say
r
is
generic
if we have
\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}^{-1}\notin\{1,p^{[K_{0}:{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}]}\}
for all
i\neq j
. This notion is independent of the choice of
\tau_{0}
.

If
r
is crystalline generic, we fix an ordering
\underline{\varphi}=(\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{n})
of the Frobenius eigenvalues. Our goal now is to describe the classification of points on
X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{r})
with representation
r
. Let
\lambda=(\operatorname{HT}_{\tau}(r))_{\tau}=(\lambda_{\tau,1},\dots,\lambda_{\tau,n})_{\tau:K\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}}
be the dominant vector of Hodge–Tate weights, such that the distinct weights appear with multiplicities
m_{\tau,1},\dots,m_{\tau,s_{\tau}}
. Let
W_{\operatorname{stab}}\subset W
be the stabilizer subgroup of
\lambda
. Then for any
w\in W/W_{\operatorname{stab}}
, we consider a character of
(K^{\times})^{n}
, denoted by
z^{-w(\lambda)}\operatorname{unr}(\underline{\varphi})
, with components given by

(z^{-w(\lambda)_{1}}\operatorname{unr}(\varphi_{1}),\dots,z^{-w(\lambda)_{n}}\operatorname{unr}(\varphi_{n}))\in\widehat{T_{L}^{n}}

Let
x_{w}\in X_{\overline{r}}^{\square}\times\widehat{T_{L}^{n}}
be the point
x_{w}=(r,z^{-w(\lambda)}\operatorname{unr}(\underline{\varphi})).
Associated to the point
x:=x_{w_{0}}\in U_{\operatorname{tri}}(\overline{r})
we can construct a certain permutation
w_{x}\in(S_{n})^{[K:{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}]}
(see
[
Wu24
, §4.1]
for a brief summary).

Theorem 6.14

(
[
BHS19
, Theorem 4.2.3]
,
[
Wu24
, Theorem 4.1]
)
.

x_{w}\in X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{r})
iff
w\geq w_{x}
in
W/W_{\operatorname{stab}}
. In fact, the set of
(r,\delta^{\prime})\in X^{\square}(\overline{r})\times\widehat{T}_{L}
such that
(r,\delta^{\prime})\in X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{r})
is precisely

\bigcup_{\mathcal{R}=\underline{\varphi}}\{(r,z^{-w(\lambda)}\operatorname{unr}(\underline{\varphi}):w_{x}\leq w\},

where the union runs over all possible orderings
\mathcal{R}
of
\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{n}
, i.e.,
refinements
.

Corollary 6.15
.

For any
i\geq 0
, if
x=((r_{\nu},\delta_{\nu}),(r_{\nu^{c}},\delta_{\nu^{c}}))\in X^{\square,n}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu})\times\widehat{T}_{L}\times X^{\square,n}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu^{c}})\times\widehat{T}_{L}
is such that
r_{\nu},r_{\nu^{c}}
are crystalline generic, then there exists an algebraic Hecke character
\chi:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to\overline{{\mathbf{Z}}}_{p}^{\times}
such that if
d_{w,\chi,\overline{\nu}}(x)\in X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi),\nu}\overline{\chi}_{\nu}^{-1})
, then
x\in X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu})\times X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu})
.

Proof.

First, we claim we can choose a crystalline character
\chi:\operatorname{Gal}_{F}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{L}^{\times}
(enlarging
L
if necessary) such that
r_{\nu}\oplus(\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}})^{-1}r_{\nu^{c}}^{\vee}(1-2n)
is also crystalline generic and the representations
r_{\nu},(\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}})^{-1}r_{\nu^{c}}^{\vee}(1-2n)
pairwise share no Hodge–Tate weights in common. The statement about Hodge–Tate weights follows from letting the weight of
(\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}})^{-1}
be very large, and the statement about genericity amounts to choosing
\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}}
to have crystalline Frobenius avoiding a finite list of elements of some p-adic field
L
, which we can ensure holds by taking the field of rationality of the Frobenius to be sufficiently large. Let
\mathbf{h}
be the dominant vector of Hodge–Tate weights for
\rho_{\nu}
,
a_{\chi}:=(-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}}),\dots,-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau}(\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}}))_{\tau}\in({\mathbf{Z}}^{n})^{\operatorname{Hom}(F_{\nu},\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})}
, and for any
k\in{\mathbf{Z}}
write
(\underline{k})=(k,\dots,k)\in({\mathbf{Z}}^{n})^{\operatorname{Hom}(F_{\nu},\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})}
. Moreover, we may also choose
\chi
so that
(\mathbf{h}^{c,\vee}+a_{\chi}+\underline{(2n-1)},\mathbf{h})\in({\mathbf{Z}}^{2n})^{\operatorname{Hom}(F_{\overline{\nu}}^{+},\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})}
is a dominant vector. Now suppose
d_{w,\chi,\overline{\nu}}(x)\in X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi),\nu}\overline{\chi_{\nu}}^{-1})
. Letting
R_{r_{\nu}},R_{(\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}})^{-1}r_{\nu^{c}}^{\vee}}
be refinements for
r_{\nu},(\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}})^{-1}r_{\nu^{c}}^{\vee}
, we let
\varphi_{\chi}=w^{-1}(\mathcal{R}_{r_{\nu}},\mathcal{R}_{(\chi_{\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}})^{-1}r_{\nu^{c}}}^{\vee})
be a refinement ordered according to the fixed
w\in W^{\overline{P}}
. We can then apply
Theorem

6.14
to
d_{w,\chi,\overline{\nu}}(x)
to write
d_{w,\chi,\overline{\nu}}(x)=(r_{\nu}\oplus(\chi_{i,\nu}\chi_{\nu^{c}})^{-1}r_{\nu^{c}}^{\vee}(1-2n),z^{-w^{\prime}(\mathbf{h}^{c,\vee}+a_{\chi}+\underline{(2n-1)},\mathbf{h})}\operatorname{unr}(\varphi_{\chi}))
for
\mathbf{h}\in({\mathbf{Z}}^{n})^{\operatorname{Hom}(F_{\nu},\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})}
, for some
w^{\prime}\geq w_{\varphi_{\chi}}
. By definition of
d_{w,\chi,\overline{\nu}}
, and the assumption on pairwise sharing no Hodge–Tate weights, we have
w^{\prime}\in w^{-1}(S_{n}\times S_{n})^{\operatorname{Hom}(F_{\overline{\nu}}^{+},\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})}
. On the other hand by construction we have
w_{\varphi_{\chi}}\in(w)^{-1}(S_{n}\times S_{n})^{\operatorname{Hom}(F_{\overline{\nu}}^{+},\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})}
. Thus writing out
w^{\prime}=w^{-1}(w_{1},w_{2})
and
w_{\mathcal{R}}=(w)^{-1}(w_{r_{\nu}},w_{r_{\nu^{c}}^{\vee}})
, we claim the inequality
w^{\prime}\geq w_{\mathcal{R}}
implies
w_{1}\geq w_{r_{\nu}}
. Indeed, this follows from the fact that
w\in W^{\overline{P}}
is a minimal length representative. Then another application of
Theorem

6.14
implies that
(r,z^{-w_{1}(\mathbf{h})}\operatorname{unr}(\varphi))\in X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu})
, as desired. The same argument works for
r_{\nu^{c}}^{\vee}
.
∎

An extension of significant interest would be to show if there is a Zariski open neighborhood
d_{w,\chi,\overline{\nu}}^{-1}(X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi),\nu}(\overline{\chi}_{\nu}^{-1}))\supset U\ni x
such that
U\subset X_{\operatorname{tri}}^{\square}(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m},\nu}).

In particular, at regular crystalline points then one could attempt to locally model eigenvarieties as closed subvarieties of the trianguline local models constructed in
[
BHS19
]
.
